<span id="page-19-2"></span><span id="page-19-1"></span>also a challenging issue. Therefore, different from [\[219\]](#page-57-7), the authors in [\[220\]](#page-57-8) propose a task offloading framework under the cloud-edge cooperative network aim of minimizing the latency of the task assignment of mobile terminal devices. Then, they propose a DQN-based algorithm to make offloading decisions, and verify the effectiveness of the proposed task offloading framework. When deploying intrusion detection systems at the edge of the network, serious packet loss issues can occur. To solve the problem, in [\[221\]](#page-57-9), the authors propose a collaborative intrusion detection system for mobile edge computing, which can transfer some detection tasks to other edge nodes with rich resources. Then, the intrusion detection task offloading problem is described as an MDP and propose a DQN-based algorithm to tackle the problem, extensive experiments show that the proposed scheme superiors to baseline schemes in terms of energy consumption, response time and packet loss rate. Different from above works, in [\[222\]](#page-57-10), the authors study the issue of how to reasonably utilize network resources in order to achieve the best resource utilization under limited communication and computing capabilities. Firstly, the authors propose a novel edge-terminal collaboration model. In the proposed model, they consider the terminal device as an autonomous vehicle, and the edge devices are roadside units with edge computing capabilities and are deployed on the roadside. Roadside units can provide vehicles with the ability to task offload, and vehicles can also collaborate with roadside units to provide computing capacity. Aiming to minimize the data processing cost and reduce latency, the author further models the problem as a DRL problem, and proposes a DQN algorithm to solve the problem. Extensive simulation results indicate the effectiveness of the proposed scheme in reducing cost and latency.

<span id="page-19-4"></span><span id="page-19-3"></span>In [\[223\]](#page-57-11), the authors consider the computation offloading problem in MEC from the perspective of computational cost and user privacy. Firstly, the authors establish a QoS model based on latency and energy consumption. Then, they propose a DDQN-based task offloading algorithm to improve the Quality of Service (QoS). At the same time, the optimal offloading strategy can be obtained based on local node state information within the coherent time of the channel, thereby solving the problem of the transmission channel changing over time and also protecting the data security of other nodes. Extensive experimental results indicate that the DDQN algorithm can achieve a higher QoS compared with existing schemes. Different from [\[223\]](#page-57-11), the authors in [\[188\]](#page-56-20) not only take the time-varying characteristics of communication channels into account, but also consider the randomness of user requests. In this context, they propose a two-stage DRL algorithm to tackle the task offloading issue in the UAV-assisted mobile edge computing system to maximize the long term utility. The simulation results show that the proposed DRL based scheme can achieve good performance in UAV assisted MEC system. In [\[224\]](#page-57-12), the authors consider how to efficiently offload computationally intensive tasks in congested situations. Firstly, they establish a multi-vehicle dynamic offloading model to minimize the total waiting delay of the total moving vehicles' tasks. Then, they divide the tasks into sequential subtasks and find the optimal solution for subtask offloading with the assistance of DDQN algorithm. Experiments results demonstrate that the DDQN algorithm is effectiveness than baseline solutions.

<span id="page-20-1"></span>The authors in [\[225\]](#page-57-13) propose a deep learning task scheduling framework and formulate the task scheduling problem as a delay cost minimization problem and prove the problem is a NP-hard problem. Then, considering the limited computation capability of edge nodes, they propose an edge-cloud collaboration system to solve the problem based on an actor-critic algorithm. Numerical results indicate that the proposed scheme can reduce the task processing time by 28.3%. In [\[226\]](#page-57-14), the author proposes a three-tier MEC network architecture. The computing tasks in this system can be processed at the edge servers and cloud servers. Meanwhile, considering that randomness of the number of tasks and environmental states, traditional optimization methods are difficult to obtain effective decisions in such dynamic network environments. Therefore, the author proposes an optimization framework based on an actor-critic algorithm to minimize the task delay. Experimental results indicate that the proposed method outperforms the baseline scheme in terms of offloading efficiency. Different from the above work, the authors in [\[227\]](#page-57-15) not only consider the end edge collaborative computing offloading problem, but also consider the service cache problem. Specifically, on the one hand, users pay fees to the cloud server to obtain computation offloading services, and on the other hand, the cloud server provides services to users and charges service fees. Then, the offloading optimization problem is novel expressed as the problem of minimizing cloud server costs while satisfying user latency requirements. Due to the NP-hard of this problem, an A3C-based optimization algorithm is proposed and simulation results indicate that the proposed method outperforms other baseline solutions.

<span id="page-20-5"></span><span id="page-20-4"></span>In [\[228\]](#page-57-16), the authors propose a task offloading scheme in end-edge-cloud orchestrated computing framework to decide when and where to offload the task which takes the mobility of edge servers and the network conditions into account. The goal is to enhance the efficiency and responsiveness of the system by dynamically allocating resources based on the user's mobility, network connectivity, and computational requirements. Then, considering the high dimensional and coupled variables, they propose a DDPG-based offloading approach to solve the task offloading problem. Finally, extensive experiments indicate that the proposed approach is superior to other baseline solutions. In [\[229\]](#page-57-17), the authors propose a novel approach to optimize computation offloading and service caching in MEC environments and explore the benefits of caching frequently used services at the edge servers to reduce latency and improve the overall efficiency of the system. Different from the work in [\[228\]](#page-57-16), the authors in this work also consider the benefit of caching popular services at the network edge. Through caching popular services at the network edge, the repeated computations and traffic can be reduced. Then, in order to minimize the cost of mobile users, the authors formulate the task offloading problem as a MINLP and propose a DDPGbased offloading algorithm with intelligent caching decisions to achieve the near-optimal solution. Finally, the proposed algorithm can enhance efficiency and reduce the response time.

<span id="page-20-7"></span><span id="page-20-6"></span><span id="page-20-3"></span><span id="page-20-2"></span><span id="page-20-0"></span>The authors in [\[230\]](#page-57-18) formulate the task offloading problem as a POMDP and propose a DQN algorithm to make the optimal offloading decisions. The novelty of this paper lies in, on the one hand, in practical systems, mobile devices only can rely on partial state information to make offloading decisions, therefore, the authors model the offloading problem as a POMDP problem. On the other hand, the author uses motion prediction to predict the future location of mobile users. By using historical data and machine learning techniques, the system can predict where users may arrive at the next time slot. This prediction enables the MEC system to proactively offload tasks, and ensures that the tasks are offloaded to edge servers which are geographically closer to the user's future location, thereby further reducing service latency. The real-world experiment results show that the scheme is better than baseline scheme at the total cost, delay and energy consumption. The authors in [\[231\]](#page-57-19) investigate the blockchain-enabled CETCN and formulate the task offloading problem as a POMDP. The key novelty of this paper lies in integrating blockchain as a decentralized and secure ledger to facilitate the task offloading process. The blockchain can enable computing nodes to transparently and tamper-resistantly record task requests, available resources, and execution results, and thus ensure trust and accountability in the offloading process. Through introducing smart contracts and token-based incentives, on the one hand, the interaction among participants can be managed and the fair resource allocation can be promised. On the other hand, the incentive mechanism can also improve the cloud-edge collaborative system's efficiency. At the same time, the proposed blockchain enabled end-edge-cloud collaborative system allows the every participant apply DRL based approach to make task offloading decisions. Finally, numerical results indicate that the algorithm can achieve a better performance <span id="page-21-0"></span>in many aspects. In [\[232\]](#page-57-20), the authors investigate the task offloading problem in UDN-assisted MEC system. Ultra-Dense Networks (UDNs) refer to a wireless communication environment with a high density of small cells, such as urban areas or crowded locations. In such scenarios, efficient task offloading strategy is crucial to optimize resource utilization and enhance network performance. Firstly, the authors propose an intelligent and adaptive task offloading method which can handle the uncertain and incomplete information of the UDNs, including network conditions, user demands, and resource availability information. Besides, the online nature of the offloading strategy allows it to adapt to dynamic changes of the UDN environment, and thus making it suitable for real-time decision-making in dynamic and evolving scenarios. Finally, extensive numerical results show that the proposed method outperforms other baseline solutions at energy consumption. Different from above works, the authors in [\[233\]](#page-57-21) propose a game-theoretic framework to help mobile users to decide where to offload their tasks. In this work, each mobile user acts as an independent player to maximize their own utility based on energy efficiency, task delay and cost. The gametheoretic approach allows for a competitive environment where mobile users and edge servers aim to maximize their individual utilities while considering the actions of other players. This enables the system to achieve a balance between offloading tasks to alleviate network congestion and executing locally to reduce latency and energy consumption. Then, they design an efficient task offloading model based on POMDP and propose a PPO-based algorithm to guide the network entities to obtain the optimal solutions. Simulation results indicate the proposed method outperforms other baseline solutions in terms of system efficiency, user satisfaction and resource utilization.

<span id="page-21-7"></span><span id="page-21-6"></span><span id="page-21-3"></span><span id="page-21-2"></span>On the one hand, the task offloading scheme based on DDPG cannot guarantee satisfying time constraints when dealing with problems with strict deadlines. On the other hand, existing schemes assume that each decision time slot has all information about the state. However, in practical problems, many real environments involve unobservable states. Huang et al. [\[234\]](#page-57-22) propose a multi-layer offloading scheme based on partially observable Markov models. This scheme can satisfy the time constraints by offloading a specific set of tasks to edge servers, while minimizing the total energy consumption of mobile devices. In addition, in order to minimize the energy consumption of the algorithm, they propose an overall decision-making mechanism for tasks that arrive within a fixed time interval. Under this mechanism, the number of calls of the DRL algorithm can be significantly reduced. The authors in [\[235\]](#page-57-23) study the air access network combining satellite, UAV communication and mobile edge computing. The novelty of this article lies in the fact that the tasks of ground equipment can be offloaded to aerial platforms, and non orthogonal multiple access technology is adopted to improve transmission rates. Secondly, due to the fact that high and low altitude platforms can only observe partial state information, and the offloading problem is a mixed nonlinear integer programming problem, the author models the problem as a partially observable Markov model and designs a MADRL algorithm to solve the offloading problem. Finally, extensive numerical results indicate that the proposed scheme can reduce the task processing delay of all the devices.

<span id="page-21-5"></span><span id="page-21-4"></span><span id="page-21-1"></span>Blockchain has been used to ensure the transaction security of mobile commerce. However, the intensive computing during the mining process limits the participation of mobile devices. The authors in [\[236\]](#page-57-24) investigate a multi-server task offloading problem with different capabilities and prices. Firstly, due to the heterogeneity of capability and price, the offloading decisions of miners are quite complex. Secondly, the longterm benefits of miners need to be maximized. Thirdly, due to the limited range of wireless communication, miners can only observe limited information about the environment. Considering the above challenges, the authors first model the task offloading problem as a Markov game to maximize the long-term benefits of miners. In addition, due to the dependence of miners' decisions on those of other miners, the decision behavior of miners is approximated as an average effect by introducing a mean field term. Based on the actor critic framework and mean field theory, each miner can be trained and executed in a distributed manner to obtain the optimal task offloading decisions. The numerical results demonstrate that the algorithm outperforms other baseline solutions at average miner utilities and convergence speeds. Wu et al. in [\[237\]](#page-57-25) proposes a DRL algorithm to address the dynamic resource allocation problem. The key contribution of the paper is to use DRL algorithm to learn an optimal policy for resource allocation. To be specific, the agent takes the current state of the system into account, such as available resources and task demands, and takes actions to allocate computation tasks between the mobile device and the edge server to optimize latency and energy efficiency based on the system state. Considering the partial observability of the system and the high dimension of variables, the authors model the task offloading problem as a dec-POMDP problem and propose an actor-critic based algorithm to obtain the task offloading decisions. The simulation result indicates the proposed algorithm is better than PPO algorithm and DDPG algorithm in terms of minimizing computational latency and energy consumption. The authors in [\[238\]](#page-57-26) consider privacy and security issues during the task offloading process. Unlike the above work, they adopt independent reinforcement learning to solve the task offloading problem under completely decentralized conditions, thereby avoiding mobile devices from exposing their local information. In order to solve the non-stationary problems faced by independent reinforcement learning, the author proposes a novel neural fictitious self-play architecture. This architecture solves non-stationary problems through a built-in self play mechanism, while utilizing gated recurrent unit to handle partial observability of the system under completely decentralized conditions. Through a large number of simulation experiments, it has been verified that the proposed method is superior to the original independent reinforcement learning. In [\[239\]](#page-57-27), the distillation module is introduced to improve the learning efficiency of some observable edge computing. Firstly, the offloading problem with time constraints is modelled as a decentralized observable Markov decision process with distillation, where each edge server makes offloading decisions based on its own observation learning strategy. This method avoids overfitting by observing historical distillation and trusting distillation. At the same time, this method can learn appropriate offloading decisions in partially observable systems to reduce communication costs. Extensive simulation results demonstrate the proposed method achieve a high offloading efficiency compared to traditional DRL methods.

*2) Resource Allocation:* After offloading tasks, the servers must decide how much computing, communication and storage resources should be allocated to the related services. Resource allocation schemes based on cloud computing or edge computing have been widely studied in academia and industry [\[240\]](#page-57-28), [\[241\]](#page-57-29), [\[242\]](#page-57-30), [\[243\]](#page-57-31), [\[244\]](#page-57-32), [\[245\]](#page-57-33), [\[246\]](#page-57-34), [\[247\]](#page-57-35), [\[248\]](#page-57-36). At the same time, the development of DRL has brought new opportunities for resource allocation under CETCN.

<span id="page-22-2"></span><span id="page-22-0"></span>With the explosive growth of mobile data, centralized cloud computing models have become an obstacle to support the continuous exchange of information generated by large-scale IoT devices. By introducing edge computing and software defined networks, these problems can be effectively alleviated. However, cloud edge collaborative networks still face with problems such as low utilization of edge resources and terminal energy. Therefore, the authors in [\[249\]](#page-57-37) propose a joint communication and computing resource allocation mechanism based on reinforcement learning to optimize processing latency under energy constraints. The simulation results show that this scheme can minimize long-term average energy consumption while maintaining low latency. In Edge-IoT networks, devices are interconnected and process data at the network edge and traditional resource allocation methods may struggle to provide optimal solutions. Therefore, the authors in [\[250\]](#page-57-38) propose an enhanced Q-learning scheme to dynamically adapt resource allocation decisions over time. This adaptability enables the network to respond changing conditions and varying device requirements effectively. By employing an online Q-learning approach, the scheme can continuously learn and refine resource allocation strategies based on real-time observations and interactions within the network. This dynamic optimization approach can achieve a balance between satisfying the individual needs of devices and maintaining fairness across the network. Experimental results indicate that the proposed scheme can improve the utility of applications while also ensuring a fair distribution of resources. In [\[251\]](#page-57-39), the authors propose a novel approach to optimize resources in a terminal-edge-cloud collaborative computing system. The key purpose of the proposed approach is to achieve efficient task offloading and resource allocation strategy. By using Q-learning, the system continuously refines its decision-making process through taking computational capabilities, network conditions, and energy constraints into account. This dynamic optimization seeks to improve overall system performance and resource utilization. Simulation results indicate the proposed method outperforms traditional methods in terms of task completion time and energy consumption.

<span id="page-22-9"></span><span id="page-22-4"></span><span id="page-22-3"></span>The authors in [\[252\]](#page-57-40) address the challenges of optimizing computation offloading and resource allocation in a heterogeneous network consisting of both cloud and edge computing <span id="page-22-5"></span>resources. In the proposed cloud-edge heterogeneous network, devices can offload computation tasks either to centralized cloud servers or to edge nodes located closer to the user. This work focuses on efficiently distributing computation tasks among available resources, and the central objective is to minimize task completion time and energy consumption. Experimental results indicate that the proposed scheme can enhance overall network performance, reduce latency, and improve resource utilization in cloud-edge computing scenarios. In [\[253\]](#page-57-41), a network resource allocation optimization problem is proposed in a cooperative UAV-assisted edge computing framework. The key purpose of the proposed strategy is to maximize the utilization of available resources while minimizing latency and energy consumption. Additionally, the paper also outlines strategies for enhancing communication and coordination among UAVs to ensure efficient task execution and data processing. The simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm outperforms other baseline solutions. In [\[254\]](#page-57-42), the authors address the challenges of content distribution in F-RANs by leveraging deep reinforcement learning. The proposed approach involves training DQN algorithm to make resource allocation decisions that maximizing the efficiency of content distribution and resource utilization while minimizing latency.

<span id="page-22-8"></span><span id="page-22-7"></span><span id="page-22-6"></span><span id="page-22-1"></span>With the continuous growth of devices, slicing, and various wireless access methods, the optimal offloading problem has become increasingly complex. In [\[255\]](#page-58-0), the author considers binary offloading and partial offloading problem in MEC system. Through utilizing stochastic game theory, the dynamic task offloading problem is modelled as a game between mobile users, with the aim of maximizing bit rate while minimizing energy consumption. At the same time, in order to alleviate the problem of overly optimistic estimation of the Q function, the authors adopt the DDQN algorithm. Through utilizing the DDQN algorithm, the optimal offloading decision can be obtained. The authors in [\[256\]](#page-58-1) propose a collaborative cloud-edge computing framework to provide low latency services and alleviate the network congestion. Firstly, in order to minimize the weighted sum cost of the system, they consider the resource allocation problem under the timevarying channel states, bandwidth constraints, and random load arrival constraints. Then, in order to minimize the latency and maximize the bandwidth utilization, the authors model the resource allocation problem as an MDP and propose a DRL based algorithm to solve the optimization problem. In addition, the novelty of this article lies in adopting circular encryption method to solve the security issue of data leakage. Finally, extensive experiment results indicate the scheme can reduce the average service latency compared to baseline solutions. Due to the severe network congestion caused by offloading a large amount of training data to cloud servers, MEC can serve as a supplement of cloud computing to reduce service latency caused by cloud computing. Therefore, the authors in [\[257\]](#page-58-2) propose an intelligent resource allocation model based on DDQN to solve the proble of limited resources and complex and ever-changing environment of MEC. In this model, they creatively introduce a federated learning framework to reduce the amount of data uploaded over wireless channels and protect data privacy. The experimental results show that this scheme outperforms traditional methods in minimizing system energy consumption and service delay.

Although MEC can improve the service experience of users, its computing offload still faces many problems, such as security and privacy issues, collaborative computing issues, and dynamic optimization issues. Therefore, in order to address the above issues, the author in [\[258\]](#page-58-3) proposes a blockchain enabled computing offloading scheme, where blockchain technology is used to ensure the reliability and irreversibility of data in MEC systems. Then, through optimizing offloading decisions, power allocation, and block size, the throughput of the MEC system can be maximized. In addition, considering the fading characteristics of wireless channels, the author transforms the above optimization problem to an MDP and proposes an algorithm based on A3C to solve the MDP problem. The final results indicate that this method outperforms existing schemes in terms of convergence speed and total rewards. In order to satisfy the extreme strict demands of users for low latency services in the Internet of Vehicles (IoV), the authors in [\[259\]](#page-58-4) propose a learning based mobile vehicle service framework. They model the dynamic changing process of the vehicle network as a Markov process, and propose a high performance A3C algorithm to solve complex resource allocation problems. This method uses SDN and ICN to dynamically orchestrate communication resources and computation resource to enhance the performance of wireless network. Finally, a large number of simulation experiments have shown that the algorithm converges quickly and improves the total compensation of network operators.

<span id="page-23-8"></span><span id="page-23-4"></span><span id="page-23-3"></span><span id="page-23-2"></span><span id="page-23-1"></span>In [\[260\]](#page-58-5), the authors consider a simple task offloading model. In this model, both task volume and time constraints need to be considered simultaneously. In order to handle these tasks reasonably, the authors propose a cloud edge collaborative multi-resource model to handle resource constrained large-scale user requests. Meanwhile, due to the inability of traditional discrete space based methods to scale well in complex environments, they propose a DDPG algorithm for continuous action space to solve the multi-resource allocation problem. In this algorithm, the authors use convolutional residual blocks, gated recursive units, and attention layers to replace fully connected networks to extract features. The simulation results show that the proposed algorithm can reduce operating costs, improve service quality, and improve the convergence speed of the algorithm. Unlike the above work, the authors in [\[261\]](#page-58-6) consider the joint optimization of task offloading and resource allocation to minimize the long-term latency and energy consumption of mobile devices. Due to the differences in decision-making between task offloading and resource allocation, the author first used the A2C algorithm to solve the task offloading problem, and then used the DDPG algorithm to solve the resource allocation problem. By alternately using these two algorithms, the optimal solution can be effectively obtained. Finally, experiment results the performance of the DDPG algorithm is outperform other existing resource allocation schemes. The authors in [\[262\]](#page-58-7) consider the resource allocation problem of communication and computing fusion in both single and multi-base station scenarios. In single base station scenario, an optimization framework based on DRL is proposed to synergistically optimize task scheduling, transmission power, and computing power. In multi-base station scenario, in order to reduce interference between base stations, a MADDPG algorithm is proposed to minimize total energy consumption and latency. The simulation results show that the proposed method outperforms the baseline scheme while considering interference, transmission power, and the computing power of the base station.

<span id="page-23-7"></span><span id="page-23-6"></span><span id="page-23-5"></span><span id="page-23-0"></span>The authors in [\[263\]](#page-58-8) investigate the joint offloading and resource allocation problem of heterogeneous tasks of multiple fog nodes. The heterogeneity of tasks includes two aspects: heterogeneity of service quality requirements and heterogeneity of resource requirements. Then, this problem is described as a stochastic game with partially observable characteristics, where each fog node can cooperate to maximize rewards, and at the same time, each fog node can only access local observations. Compared with the joint action learning scheme, this scheme greatly reduces feedback and memory consumption. At the same time, in order to solve the problem of partial observability, the author adopted a deep recursive Q-network to approximate the optimal value function. Finally, the numerical results indicate that the proposed algorithm has a higher average success rate and lower average overflow compared to the baseline scheme. Due to the workload of users accessing edge networks and the uncertainty of available resources of edge nodes, the optimal resource allocation problem in edge networks faces significant challenges. The author in [\[264\]](#page-58-9) proposes a computational offloading and resource allocation scheme to minimize computational energy consumption. Unlike the work in [\[263\]](#page-58-8), the author considers nonoffloadable tasks and offloadable tasks. Compared to nonoffloadable tasks, offloadable tasks can be processed at multiple nodes in the network. Due to the uncertainty of edge node resources, the author models the task offloading problem as a stochastic game with the goal of maximizing the overall performance of the system. Compared with traditional methods, the method proposed by the author can effectively reduce computational energy consumption while ensuring convergence. In [\[265\]](#page-58-10), the author considers the issue of using DRL to achieve task offloading decisions while improving energy efficiency in green MEC scenarios. Firstly, the author decouples the problem into two sub problems: subcarrier allocation problem and power control problem. In order to improve practicality, each MEC server can only obtain partial information of the heterogeneous network. Then an optimization algorithm based on the PPO framework is proposed to achieve efficient resource allocation. The final simulation results show that the algorithm outperforms the baseline scheme in terms of effectiveness, robustness, and scalability. Different from above works, the authors in [\[266\]](#page-58-11) consider the sequential decisionmaking problem of user association and resource allocation in MEC scenarios to maximize the average completion rate of tasks with interdependent relationships. Due to the fact that edge servers can only observe the computing tasks, communication, and computing resources within their coverage range, the author models this problem as a Dec-POMDP. In this <span id="page-24-2"></span>model, edge servers make decentralized decisions based on user association, resource management and local information of their network status. Considering the continuous property of variables, a multi-agent DDPG algorithm is developed to solve this problem. The simulation results show that this algorithm can effectively improve the completion rate of tasks. The authors in [\[267\]](#page-58-12) consider the problem of computing offloading in ultra dense networks, with the aim of minimizing the computational cost of the system while considering user energy consumption and latency. The novelty of this article lies in the combination of DRL and mean field game theory, which not only effectively solves the problem of traditional methods difficult to model complex environments, but also solves the decision-making behavior of dense users, thus reduces the complexity of the problem.

## <span id="page-24-1"></span>*D. Lesson Learned and Future Directions*

It has been shown that in recent years, ML has been proposed to optimize the task offloading and resource allocation decisions, especially the DRL. Since DRL can provide a better approach for the large dimension, distributed and dynamic aspect of CETCN, it plays a more and more critical role in improving the effectiveness of the CETCN. Indeed, DRL provides a better solution in a short time, which satisfies the requirements of time-intensive applications. Table [III](#page-16-2) summarized in this survey indicates that a lot of efforts has been put to investigate the task offloading and resource allocation issues. Moreover, most studies focus on cost, latency, and energy consumption but have not investigated them in a unified framework. Additionally, different optimization models focus on different objectives and it is necessary to provide a solution that optimize as many objectives as possible and then provide a unified criterion to measure the performance in the CETCN system.

It is essential to note that there are still several open issues in the task offloading and resource allocation problems that need to be investigated by the research community. Indeed, it is a challenge to allocate resources at a lower cost under the constraints of delay and energy. Furthermore, mobile devices may lose their connectivity because of the users' mobility, especially in the intelligent transportation system. Therefore, the offloading scheme should design fault-tolerant mechanisms to resend the lost data or design flexible mobility management mechanisms to adapt to the occurrence of the mistakes. Last but not least, privacy protection issue is still the focus of users' attention. The privacy protection mechanism based on edge computing is not mature, mainly due to the limited computing power of edge nodes. Therefore, cloud edge collaborative privacy protection mechanism still needs to be carefully designed.

We list the several most leading research directions in the task offloading and resource allocation problems as follows:

• *Dynamic Workloads:* Plenty of researches have considered the static scenarios of offloading. Due to the dynamic of the device mobility and channel state, more sophisticated offloading strategies need to be designed to adapt to the dynamic characteristics.

- *Real-world Trace:* Most proposed task offloading and resource allocation schemes are demonstrated based on numerical and simulation analysis. Many of them simplify the models of the real world, for example, simplified wireless channel models [\[268\]](#page-58-13), [\[269\]](#page-58-14), [\[270\]](#page-58-15). Hence, it is necessary to consider more practical and realistic scenarios evaluated by real-world traces such as [\[253\]](#page-57-41).
- <span id="page-24-3"></span>• *Federated Learning Offloading:* Federated learning allows devices to collaboratively train models while keeping data localized. Future research may focus on enhancing federated learning techniques for offloading and aggregating model updates efficiently across edge devices, ensuring privacy and accuracy.
- *Green and Energy-Efficient Offloading:* Given the increasing importance of sustainability, future directions might involve incorporating energy-aware algorithms that optimize offloading decisions to reduce the carbon footprint of computing tasks.
- *6G and Network Slicing:* The rollout of 6G networks and network slicing capabilities can enable more dynamic and fine-grained control over network resources. Future research might explore how to exploit these capabilities for efficient task offloading based on real-time network conditions.
- *Security and Privacy-Enhanced Offloading:* With growing concerns about data privacy and security, future research should focus on developing secure protocols for offloading sensitive tasks and data, ensuring end-to-end encryption and authentication.
- *Autonomous Resource Management:* Develop selfadaptive algorithms that enable edge devices and cloud resources to autonomously allocate and adjust resources based on real-time data, workload changes, and performance metrics.
- *Predictive Analytics:* Utilize predictive analytics and machine learning to forecast resource demands and trends, allowing for proactive resource allocation adjustments and optimization.
- *Quality of Experience (QoE) Optimization:* Shift focus from mere resource allocation to optimizing the overall user experience by considering subjective factors like user satisfaction, application responsiveness, and perceived quality.
- *Multi-Objective Resource Allocation:* Consider multiple conflicting objectives, such as performance, energy efficiency, cost, and user satisfaction, and develop algorithms that find Pareto-optimal solutions for diverse use cases.

## <span id="page-24-0"></span>VI. COLLABORATIVE CACHING FOR AI-ENABLED CLOUD-EDGE-TERMINAL COLLABORATIVE NETWORK

Caching at the CETCN has attracted increasing attention from academia and industry due to its advantages in reducing content or service acquisition delay and relieving the traffic pressure of the core network. Hence, in this section, we firstly introduce caching model in the CETCN in Section [VI-A.](#page-25-0) In Section [VI-B,](#page-25-1) we analyse the MEC from the perspective of computer science. Then, in Section [VI-C,](#page-26-0) we investigate the

![](_page_25_Figure_2.jpeg)

<span id="page-25-2"></span>Fig. 8. A toy example of cooperative caching.

works related to content caching and service caching in the CETCN. Finally, we point out several remaining challenges and possible future directions in Section VI-D.

#### <span id="page-25-0"></span>A. Collaborative Caching Model for CETCN

As shown in Fig. 8, the toy example of cooperative caching is consisted terminal layer with two users, edge layer with two base stations which equipped with caching capacity and cloud layer with data center. MEC servers in the edge layer can cooperative with each other to caching content and communicate with each other to realize content sharing.

As depicted in Fig. 8, when User 1 requests a content of size c or generates a task (the task should be processed by a service or an application which cached at MEC server or cloud center) which denoted as  $\{s,f,\tau,c\}$  in Section IV, due to the limited caching capacity of MEC servers, the process of content retrieving can be described as follows:

- The first step: The user 1 generate a content or service request to the local MEC server, if the local MEC server has the content or can process the service at the local MEC server, then, the local MEC server transmits the content or the computing result to the user 1.
- The second step: If the local MEC server does not cache
  the target content or the service, then the local MEC
  server requests the content from other MEC servers in
  the cooperative domain, and then the local MEC server
  transmits the content to the user.
- The third step: If the edge layer does not have the content or the service, the local MEC will request the content from the data center, and then transmits the content to the user through backhaul link.

It is worth noting that collaboration between users and between edge layers is not a necessary option in the actual cloud edge collaboration system.

Due to the limited caching capacity of MEC servers, for content caching which size is c or service placement which denoted by  $\{s,f,\tau,c\}$ , it must satisfy the caching capacity constraint. If we assume the caching capacity of MEC server i is  $C_i$ , then the maximum number of cached content or service should satisfy  $\sum_i^N c_i \leq C_i$ . On the one hand, the caching placement strategy need to be designed reasonably to minimize the content retrieving latency or minimize the energy consumption. On the other hand, due to caching placement problem is often NP-hard, and the traditional method often time consuming, but lucky, the DRL method can overcome this shortcoming of the traditional methods. In the following,

we will do a literature review about cooperative caching using DRL in recent years.

#### <span id="page-25-1"></span>B. Analyse MEC From Computer Science Perspective

Due to the fact that content caching works in application layer, therefore, in the following, we will analyze MEC from the perspective of application layer, including MEC fundamentals, architecture, edge algorithms, access latency and response time, SDN/NFV and edge AI.

- <span id="page-25-4"></span><span id="page-25-3"></span>1) MEC Fundamentals: The core components of the MEC architecture include edge servers, access network, mobile and IoT devices and backhaul network. Edge servers are the central processing units of MEC architecture. They are located at the edge of the network, closer to end-user devices compared with cloud server. These servers host virtual machines (VMs) or containers that run applications, services, and functions [271]. Edge servers enable real-time data processing, computation, and storage, and have quicker response time and shorter transmission distance [272]. The access network refers to the connectivity infrastructure that connects end-user devices (e.g., mobile phones, IoT devices) to edge servers. The access network ensures seamless communication between devices and edge resources [273]. Mobile devices, such as smartphones and tablets, as well as Internet of Things (IoT) devices, generate and consume data. MEC architecture places edge servers in proximity to these devices, enabling faster data processing and real-time interactions. The backhaul network connects edge servers to centralized cloud data centers. It facilitates data synchronization, backup, and offloading between the edge and the core cloud infrastructure. The backhaul network ensures that data is appropriately managed and stored.
- <span id="page-25-5"></span>2) Architecture: Through combining edge servers, access network, mobile and IoT devices and backhaul network, the MEC can provide services to users. Taking content caching as an example. Firstly, when a user on a mobile device requests content, such as a Web page or a video, the request is sent through the access network. Then, the access network routes the content request to the nearest edge server based on factors such as proximity, load, and caching policies. Thirdly, The edge server evaluates whether the requested content is already cached. If the content is present in the cache and is still valid, the edge server can directly serve the cached content to the mobile device, reducing the need to fetch it from a distant data center. This reduces latency and accelerates content delivery. Finally, the edge server periodically updates and manages the cache to ensure that the content remains fresh and relevant. It can evict outdated content or content with lower demand to make space for new content. Last but not least, backhaul connection is optional, to be specific, periodically or under specific conditions, the edge server may synchronize the cached content with the central cloud data center over the backhaul connection. This ensures that the cached content remains consistent across the MEC environment.
- 3) Edge Algorithms: Edge caching involves storing frequently accessed content or data closer to end-users at the edge of the network to reduce latency and improve content delivery. The common edge caching algorithms include least

<span id="page-26-1"></span>recently used (LRU), least frequently used (LFU), random replacement, first-in-first-out (FIFO), least recently used with frequency count (LFU-2), greedy dual-size frequency (GDSF), greedy dual-size frequency with aging (GDSF-A), weighted LRU (W-LRU) [\[274\]](#page-58-19), [\[275\]](#page-58-20). LRU is a simple algorithm that removes the least recently accessed content from the cache when it becomes full. It assumes that recently accessed content is more likely to be accessed again soon. LFU removes the least frequently accessed content from the cache. It assumes that less visited content is less likely to be accessed in the future. FIFO removes the oldest content that has been added to the cache. It's a straightforward algorithm but may not consider content popularity. LFU-2 combines elements of LRU and LFU. It tracks both recency and frequency of user's access, evicting content that is both infrequently accessed and was accessed a long time ago. GDSF considers both size and frequency of content access when making eviction decisions. It aims to optimize cache hit rates based on both factors. W-LRU assigns weights to each content based on factors like popularity or importance. When evicting content, it considers both recency and the assigned weight.

- *4) Assess Latency and Response Time:* Taking content caching as an example, edge servers can cache frequently requested content, such as videos, images, or Web pages. When a device requests cached content, it can quickly retrieve it from nearby edge servers, thereby reducing the need for long-distance data transmission from the remote cloud server [\[276\]](#page-58-21), [\[277\]](#page-58-22).
- <span id="page-26-2"></span>*5) SDN/NFV:* SDN makes the network programmable by separating the control plane and data plane. The SDN control plane is represented by software called the SDN controller, which is responsible for determining the processing of underlying data traffic. The data plane, as a network device, is responsible for forwarding data according to a set of rules. The SDN controller allows the creation and management of these rules through a programming interface in the northbound interface. The separation of data plane and control plane makes network policy execution simple, flexible, and easy to configure and develop [\[278\]](#page-58-23). Virtualization is a foundational technology in MEC, which can enable the efficient utilization of resources, dynamic scaling, and the seamless deployment of services at the edge of the network [\[279\]](#page-58-24), [\[280\]](#page-58-25). It forms the basis for achieving the flexibility and low-latency capabilities of MEC. To be specific, NFV abstracts storage resources as virtual entities that can be allocated to different services or applications at the network edge, and this abstraction can better utilize hardware resources for dynamic allocation.
- <span id="page-26-5"></span><span id="page-26-4"></span><span id="page-26-3"></span>*6) Edge AI:* Integrating AI into edge caching can further enhance the ability of MEC. Edge based artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) algorithms and frameworks are crucial for directly implementing intelligent caching decisions at network edges [\[281\]](#page-58-26), [\[282\]](#page-58-27). These algorithms and frameworks allow devices and edge servers to process data locally, thereby reducing the need to transfer data to centralized data centers. Common edge based AI/ML algorithms and frameworks include reinforcement learning, LSTM (Long Short-Term Memory) for temporal patterns, collaborative filtering, content popularity prediction and so on.

## <span id="page-26-0"></span>*C. Literature Review*

In CETCN, many scholars and researchers have always been concerned about caching. With the growth of mobile data traffic, caching content at the network edge is a promising way to release traffic congestion. Many studies have shown that 80% of the traffic consumption in the network comes from users' repeated acquisition of popular content, and popular content often accounts for only a small part of all contents. Therefore, caching popular content in advance can effectively relieve the tremendous traffic pressure on the backhaul link. At the same time, due to the limited storage resources of edge devices, how to implement distributed collaborative caching in CETCN is of great concern.

In recent years, reinforcement learning has shown its great potential to learn content popularity in complex environments. By accurately expressing content popularity, better caching decisions can be made. Therefore, using reinforcement learning to make content caching decisions is of increasing interest to researchers compared to traditional methods [\[283\]](#page-58-28), [\[284\]](#page-58-29), [\[285\]](#page-58-30). Likewise, at the end of this section, we summarize the literature on DRL-based cloudedge-terminal collaborative caching in Table [VII.](#page-27-0)

<span id="page-26-9"></span><span id="page-26-8"></span><span id="page-26-7"></span><span id="page-26-6"></span>In order to alleviate the impact of the increasing in cache types on cache performance, the authors in [\[286\]](#page-58-31) propose a collaborative caching mechanism that combines remote radio heads and baseband units. This mechanism is based on the high flexibility and scalability of SDN. Then, due to the difficulty of considering all environmental factors in traditional rule-based and heuristic methods, a Q-learning based method has been proposed for designing caching mechanisms. Finally, the author proposes an unit cache capacity hit efficiency to evaluate cache performance. Extensive experimental results indicate that the Q-learning based caching strategy can improve the system performance. In [\[287\]](#page-58-32), the authors propose a novel storage architecture. The architecture consists of edge servers and edge users, and the edge user is composed of data producer and data consumer. The edge server includes a master container and several data containers. The master container manages the data containers and assigns storage tasks to data containers. To storage data at the edge server efficiently, the master container first identifies the data's popularity, then utilize the Q-learning algorithm to determine which place to store the data. Secondly, the master node determines which data should be updated through a scalable recommendation algorithm. Finally, the maser container will clean its redundant storage content when the data containers reach its capacity. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that the proposed intelligent storage architecture is more efficient than the comparison schemes. In [\[288\]](#page-58-33), the author considers the joint allocation of cache, computing, and communication resources. Unlike the above work, this work not only considers cloud edge collaboration, but also considers the impact of traffic distribution on the popularity of cached content. The author uses queuing theory to model the content distribution process as a delay minimization problem, and then uses Q-learning to make the best caching decision to improve network content delivery and reduce latency.

| [300]     | DQN               | Terminal-edge-cloud | Minimize the access delay and traffic cost            | Simulation analysis | POMDP     |
| [301]     | MAAC              | Terminal-edge-cloud | Minimize the access delay and traffic cost            | Simulation analysis | POMDP     |
| [302]     | MADDPG            | Terminal-edge-cloud | Minimize the execution latency                        | Simulation analysis | Dec-POMDP |

<span id="page-27-1"></span>In order to meet the user's demand for multimedia service access, In [\[289\]](#page-58-34), the authors introduce D2D communication technology to the edge caching problem. Specifically, the authors propose a D2D-assisted collaborative caching framework in the heterogeneous network where flexible cooperation can be carried out between user devices, edge servers and cloud servers. Secondly, through modeling the node selection and content placement problem as an MDP, the authors propose a deep Q-learning method to solve the cooperative caching problem. Finally, extensive simulation results demonstrate the effectiveness of the method at reducing access latency of content and improving hit rate. In order to reduce the transmission delay and traffic pressure of the backbone network, the author proposes a fog node collaborative caching strategy based on DQN in [\[290\]](#page-58-35). Firstly, a topic model is used to predict user preferences to obtain the expected content popularity. Secondly, considering the coupling of variables in the optimization problem, the predicted content popularity results are combined with original content popularity to obtain the optimal content caching strategy. Finally, numerical results indicate that the proposed caching scheme can reduce the average delay. The author in [\[291\]](#page-58-36) proposes a low latency edge caching method to reduce user access latency and effectively cache content in edge networks. Firstly, the author models the cache problem between base stations as a delay minimization problem and transforms it into a maximum cache reward problem, through utilizing the greedy algorithm of origin dual interior points to obtain the strategy for the cache problem. Secondly, in order to improve service quality and balance communication and migration costs, a joint communication

and task migration cost model is established and a task transfer scheme based on reinforcement learning is proposed. Finally, a large number of comparisons are conducted to verify that the proposed caching method can reduce latency, and at the same time, the proposed migration algorithm can reduce communication overhead.

<span id="page-27-5"></span><span id="page-27-4"></span><span id="page-27-3"></span><span id="page-27-2"></span>Ren et al. [\[292\]](#page-58-37) consider the allocation of computation and cache resources between multi-level edge nodes. Users can offload computing tasks to edge servers and optimize the task offloading and resource allocation strategies of edge servers to meet their needs for low latency services. The complexity of this problem is that when computing, caching and communication resource allocation are considered at the same time, the complexity of edge computing systems will become very high. In order to solve the complex resource allocation problem of edge servers, the author proposes a DDQN algorithm, which can achieve fast decision-making while maximize long-term benefits. The experimental results show that the algorithm can effectively allocate resources in edge networks. In [\[293\]](#page-58-38), the authors propose a content caching and request routing scheme at the network edge to meet the growing demand for emerging services and propose a DDQN algorithm for cooperative content caching to minimize the content fetching delay. The difference from the above work is that the proposed method does not require any prior knowledge of the popularity distribution of the content. Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed algorithm is superior than other baseline caching algorithms. Centralized optimization methods based on learning will consume excessively network resources during training and data transmission. To address <span id="page-28-0"></span>this challenge, the authors in [\[294\]](#page-58-39) propose a new federated caching framework based on DRL. This framework takes the first round of training parameters of the base station as the initial input parameters for local training, enabling the base station to collaborate in learning shared prediction models. Then, the terminal devices upload the nearly optimal local parameters to the base station to participate in the next round of global training. By introducing federated learning, data is decoupled from the training process to achieve rapid decision-making. The simulation results show that the proposed collaborative caching framework can reduce average latency, alleviate traffic pressure on backbone networks, and improve cache hit rates.

<span id="page-28-1"></span>In [\[295\]](#page-58-40), the authors consider the cooperative content replacement problem in a realistic scenario where the content popularity is unknown to the edge servers, in order to obtain the optimal caching replacement strategy in this scenario, they design a content replacement scheme based on multi-agent AC algorithm where the reward of the agent is the saved delay, extensive numerical results indicate that the proposed cooperative content replacement algorithm can improve the cache hit ratio compared with existing schemes. Mobile edge caching can reduce access latency and redundant traffic transmission through storing content at network edges. Unlike the above work, the authors in [\[296\]](#page-58-41) consider the complex and ever-changing edge network environment, such as a large number of dynamic and diverse request patterns, and they also consider the high similarity of requests between adjacent edge nodes. In this context, they propose an intelligent edge caching framework that can provide a diverse and distributed caching environment to reduce access latency and traffic costs. At the same time, through utilizing MADRL, each node can learn the optimal caching strategy. Finally, the real-driven evaluation demonstrates that the proposed scheme outperforms other state-of-the-art caching solutions.

<span id="page-28-8"></span><span id="page-28-5"></span><span id="page-28-4"></span><span id="page-28-3"></span>In [\[297\]](#page-58-42), the authors propose an edge caching scheme based on DRL. First, the edge server determines its cache operation according to its cache state, and then the centralized server evaluates the edge server's cache decision to guide the edge server's subsequent decisions. On this basis, considering a scenario where the size of actual and reasonable data items is inconsistent, the authors propose a new multi-agent rolecriticism caching algorithm. Extensive experimental results indicate that the algorithm can promote collaboration between edge servers and improve the system hit ratio. Aiming to tackle the issue of redundancy of caching content in different regions and retransmission delays on the communication links caused by the mobility of users, Xu et al. [\[298\]](#page-58-43) propose a cooperative caching scheme based on DRL, which can realize the collaborative caching between edge servers and cloud server. Specifically, first of all, the predictive model is adopted to describe the users' content preference and movement trajectory. Secondly, the DRL algorithm is used to reduce the rate of redundant content and transmission delay. The author in [\[299\]](#page-58-44) proposes a method that utilizes the cache capacity of base stations to minimize the average latency of user request content and the energy consumption of the network. Specifically, the delay of user requests for content is mainly composed of the time spent on wireless and fiber optic links. Energy consumption mainly comes from the forwarding process of content between base stations and servers. Due to the unpredictability of network state and the huge network dimension, the author adopts the DDPG algorithm to learn the optimal caching strategy through interaction with the environment. The simulation results show that the proposed caching framework can improve the cache hit rate and reduce the energy consumption of the network.

<span id="page-28-7"></span><span id="page-28-6"></span><span id="page-28-2"></span>In order to satisfy the users' requirements for latencysensitive and computation-intensive services in IoT-enabled MEC system, the authors in [\[300\]](#page-59-0) propose a cache assisted task offloading framework that enables joint optimization of communication, caching, and computing resources for the IoT-enabled MEC system. Specifically, first of all, the application services are first pre-stored on edge servers, and thus reduce the latency cost of obtaining and initializing services. Secondly, due to the fact that each base station intelligently observes partial information of the environment and in order to minimize the latency of computation offloading and resource allocation, the author models the multi-agent decision-making problem as a POMDP. Then, they propose a graph DRL algorithm to solve the problem. This algorithm utilizes a graph neural network to obtain the relationships between nodes and inputs these relationships into a deep Q network for resource allocation decisions. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the algorithm can effectively reduce latency by optimizing resource allocation. In [\[301\]](#page-59-1), the author models the collaborative caching problem as a POMDP to jointly optimize the cost for users to obtain content from local servers, nearby servers, and remote servers. Firstly, the author designs a multi-agent actor-critic framework, in which agents reduce data interaction between agents by sharing actions. Secondly, considering the variation of content popularity over time, the author proposes a popularity estimation method based on variational recurrent neural networks, which can learn content popularity without understanding user preferences. At the same time, due to the mobility of the terminal devices, the author utilizes LSTM to simulate the user's access status. The simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme can improve the benefits of BSs. The author in [\[302\]](#page-59-2) proposes a collaborative task offloading, service caching, and transmission power allocation problem to reduce terminal computing latency and energy consumption. Unlike the above work, the author considers that obtaining all information about the network in a decentralized MEC network is impractical. Therefore, the author models the resource allocation problem as a Dec-POMDP problem, where each edge base station can cooperate to obtain the optimal solution in situations where only partial information about the network can be obtained for a typical edge server. In addition, in order to better learn cooperative information, the author introduces graph neural networks into the multi-agent reinforcement learning framework, allowing agents to selectively obtain information from other agents. Extensive simulation results demonstrate the proposed scheme outperforms other solutions at average reward, cache hit ratio, and offloading cost.

## <span id="page-29-1"></span>*D. Lesson Learned and Future Directions*

In this survey, we investigate the issue of cooperative caching in the CETCN. Taking advantage of edge computing, caching popular content to edge nodes can provide users with low-latency services. However, due to the limited capacity of edge nodes, designing a reasonable caching strategy has become the focus of academia. The traditional method of solving integer programming is computationally intensive and difficult to obtain the optimal solution, but luckily, with the further development of artificial intelligence, DRL has become a promising method for solving the problem of cooperative caching. However, there are still existing a few challenging issues that need to be solved in cooperative content caching domain. We summarize several key points as follows.

- *Towards privacy preserving intelligent caching:* Similarly, when dealing with the edge caching problem, we often assume that the entire edge network knows the content requests of all users, and uses the request information to optimize collaborative caching. However, due to users' privacy requirements, users often do not want to disclose their content preferences. Therefore, how to design a reasonable cooperative caching strategy when only knowing the content requested by the edge node itself will also become one of the future research directions.
- *Data Placement and Replication:* Determining which data should be cached at which locations and how to replicate data efficiently is a complex task. Making the right decisions about data placement and replication is crucial to ensure that frequently accessed data is available at the right location without causing excessive data duplication.
- *Dynamic Workload Variation:* The workload on the network can vary significantly over time, and cooperative caching systems need to adapt to these variations. Balancing the cache content and placement in response to changing user demands and network conditions is a non-trivial task.
- *Heterogeneous Devices:* Edge devices in a cooperative caching network can be heterogeneous in terms of their capabilities, storage capacities, and communication bandwidth. Designing caching algorithms that can efficiently utilize the varying resources of these devices is a challenge.
- *Cache Consistency and Synchronization:* Maintaining cache consistency across distributed caches is important to avoid serving stale or inconsistent data to users. Implementing efficient cache synchronization protocols that minimize overhead and guarantee data consistency is a challenge.
- *Caching from an economic vision:* Since content caching involves the interests of multiple parties, equipment providers need to provide hardware facilities that can be utilized to cache content, content providers need to cache some content in hardware system according to the popularity of these content, and operators need to provide users with necessary connection services. Users get the content they need by paying a certain fee. Therefore, how

to reasonably divide the benefits from the users will also be one of the future possible research directions.

## <span id="page-29-0"></span>VII. MOBILITY MANAGEMENT FOR AI-ENABLED CLOUD-EDGE-TERMINAL COLLABORATIVE NETWORK

The requirements of emerging applications for high throughput and low latency make mobility management more and more challenging. In the CETCN, the intensive deployment of edge base stations enables users to access multiple base stations at the same time. Although it increases the complexity of connection, the flexibility of user mobility is also increased through multi-cell connection. Therefore, in this section, we first discuss the existing mobility management standards in Section [VII-A.](#page-29-2) Then, we briefly describe an example of mobility management problem in subsection in Section [VII-B.](#page-30-0) Then, Section [VII-C](#page-31-0) reviews the efforts of researchers that have been put in this domain. Finally, in Section [VII-D,](#page-32-0) we summarize the mobility management issues in the CETCN and point out the possible research directions in the future.

## <span id="page-29-2"></span>*A. Existing Mobility Management Scheme in 3GPP standards*

In 5G, the mobility management scheme defined by the 3rd Generation Partnership Project (3GPP) standards includes several procedures and functions that enable seamless connectivity for user devices as they move within the network. The mobility management scheme in 5G builds upon the concepts and enhancements introduced in previous generations while also incorporating new features to improve performance, efficiency, and flexibility. Some key elements of the 5G mobility management scheme are as follows [\[303\]](#page-59-3), [\[304\]](#page-59-4), [\[305\]](#page-59-5):

- <span id="page-29-3"></span>• *Intra-RAT (Radio Access Technology) Handover:* Inter-RAT handover refers to the process of transferring an ongoing data session from one cell to another cell within the same type of radio network. In other words, it is a handover that occurs between two cells that are part of the same radio technology, such as one 5G NR to another 5G NR cell. Intra-RAT handover schemes include hard handover, soft handover and softer handover.
- *Inter-RAT Handover:* Inter-RAT handover schemes include inter-frequency handover and 4G to 5G handover and vice versa. 5G networks often coexist with previous-generation networks like LTE or even older technologies. Inter-RAT handover procedures allow user devices to smoothly transition between 5G and these legacy networks, ensuring continuous connectivity and service availability.
- *Idle Mode Mobility:* Idle-Mode mobility schemes include cell reselection and tracking area update. In idle mode, when a user device is not actively using the network, mobility management procedures are in place to manage tracking area updating to ensure the device can be reached efficiently when needed.
- *Xn-based Handover:* Xn-based Handover is used within the 5G New Radio (NR) architecture and involves direct communication between two gNodeBs (5G base station) using Xn interface.

![](_page_30_Picture_2.jpeg)

Fig. 9. A toy example of mobility management.

- <span id="page-30-1"></span>• *NG-based Handover:* Xn-based handover enables seamless transmission for a User Equipment (UE) between different gNodeBs.
- *Dual Connectivity:* Devices can maintain simultaneous connections with two different cells (usually one for the control plane and one for the user plane) to ensure smooth handover and better reliability.
- *Network Slicing-based Mobility Management:* With the introduction of network slicing in 5G, multiple virtual networks with different characteristics can coexist over the same physical infrastructure. Mobility management schemes must ensure that devices can smoothly move between different network slices while maintaining service continuity.
- *Hierarchical Mobility Management:* In this scheme, different layers of hierarchy in the network handle different aspects of mobility. For example, local mobility could be handled by a micro-controller closer to the edge, while broader events are managed by a central entity.
- *IP-based Mobility Management:* Mobile IP and its variants like Proxy Mobile IP can be used for IP session continuity during the handover.

In addition, the mentioned mobility management schemes can be adapted or combined to accommodate specific use cases or network conditions.

## <span id="page-30-0"></span>*B. Challenges of Mobility Management*

Mobility is an inherent attribute of many emerging applications, when a user is watching a video on a mobile device, such as a phone, and is moving around as shown in Fig. [9,](#page-30-1) several mobility management challenges can arise due to the changing network conditions and the requirements of maintaining a seamless user experience. We list specific technical problems and challenges of mobility management problems as follows [\[306\]](#page-59-6), [\[307\]](#page-59-7), [\[308\]](#page-59-8):

- <span id="page-30-2"></span>• *Handover Disruption***:** As the user moves, the device might need to switch between different cells or access points. If the handover process is not smooth and quick enough, it can lead to disruptions in the video stream, causing buffering or even disconnecting the session.
- *Handover Optimization***:** Seamless handovers between different access points and network layers are essential

- to maintain a stable connection. However, in collaborative networks, determining the optimal handover decision considering factors like signal strength, available resources, and user mobility can be complex.
- *Resource Management***:** Efficiently managing and allocating resources across terminal, edge, and cloud nodes is crucial for performance optimization. This includes managing computing resources, storage capacity, and network bandwidth, and ensuring proper load balancing.
- *Latency and Quality of Service (QoS)***:** Mobility can introduce latency variations and fluctuations in network quality. Video streaming requires consistent bandwidth and low latency to provide a good quality experience. If the network cannot maintain adequate QoS during mobility, the video quality may suffer.
- *Energy Efficiency***:** Terminal devices often have limited battery capacity. Effective mobility management should aim to minimize energy consumption during handovers and data offloading to extend the battery life of mobile devices.
- *Packet Loss and Jitter***:** Movement between cells can lead to packet loss and jitter, which can further degrade the video streaming experience. Lost packets or inconsistent packet arrival times can result in pixelation, freezing, or audio-video sync issues.
- *Load Balancing***:** When many users are simultaneously streaming videos or performing data-intensive tasks, the network needs to balance the load across different cells or sectors. Inefficient load balancing can lead to congestion and reduced performance.
- *Signal Strength and Coverage***:** Movement can affect the signal strength and coverage. In areas with weaker signals, the video stream might degrade or pause entirely. The handover process needs to ensure that the new cell provides adequate signal strength for video streaming.
- *Interference and Heterogeneous Networks***:** In urban areas or places with overlapping coverage from different technologies (e.g., 5G, Wi-Fi, LTE), interference and handover decisions become more complex. Managing handovers between different types of networks can impact video streaming.
- *Frequent Handovers***:** Rapid movement or switching between cells can lead to frequent handovers, which can put additional load on the network and impact the device's battery life.
- *Context-Aware Handover***:** The network needs to make intelligent decisions about when and where to perform handovers based on the user's context, application requirements, and available resources. This procedure requires sophisticated mobility management algorithms.

<span id="page-30-3"></span>Mobility management issue has been extensively investigated in traditional cellular networks [\[309\]](#page-59-9), [\[310\]](#page-59-10), [\[311\]](#page-59-11). In these previous works, user mobility is modeled with connection probabilities or link reliability using information such as the user's moving speed and directions. However, this simple information is not sufficient for CETCN system due to its complex, dynamic and heterogeneous environment. Addressing these challenges requires a holistic approach,
