combining advanced intelligent decision-making algorithms, and effective coordination among different network layers in terminal-edge-cloud collaborative networks. Therefore, in the following, we do an investigation about mobility management using DRL to address the mentioned challenges.

## <span id="page-31-0"></span>*C. Literature Review*

Mobility management can provide users with a ubiquitous and seamless connection. Due to the dense deployment of small base stations, traditional switch control methods will lead to various issues, such as frequent handover and failed handovers. Therefore, if the mobile terminal decides to offload tasks to the CETCN, it is very important to ensure the continuity of the business.

In general, the mobility assumption based on the Markov chain model is often oversimplified in reality [\[312\]](#page-59-12), [\[313\]](#page-59-13). In fact, the mobility of terminal devices in the real world is more complicated. Therefore, it is difficult to describe the mobility of terminal equipment with a standard mathematical model.

DRL is considered to be the most promising approach to solve the mobility management problem at present, since DRL can extract and identify hidden information from complex states, and perform optimal operations in a single framework. Therefore, in this survey, we mainly summarize the issues of mobility management and its DRL solutions in CETCN system. A summary is presented in Table [VIII.](#page-31-1)

<span id="page-31-3"></span>The authors in [\[314\]](#page-59-14) consider the issues of switching and business migration in large-scale deployment of mobile application scenarios. Firstly, they propose a mobile user trajectory prediction method that combines Q-learning and short-term memory networks, enabling the training process of the model to be automated. Then, in order to reduce the cost of predicting user trajectories with similar movement patterns, a group user trajectory predictor is developed. Finally, in order to verify the superiority of the proposed mobility management scheme, they propose a service migration scheme based on trajectory prediction. The experimental results show that the predictor <span id="page-31-5"></span><span id="page-31-4"></span>can not only improve QoE, but also effectively reduce return flow. Ultra-dense network is a promising solution to satisfy the growing demand of users' requirements, and expand the coverage of the network, but the interference, additional unnecessary handover and signaling overhead makes the quality of services worse. Hence, in [\[315\]](#page-59-15), the authors propose a Q-learning based handover optimization scheme to minimize the effect of frequent and unnecessary handovers. Extensive experiments show that the proposed algorithm can minimize the probability of radio link failure and maximize throughput compared to other methods. For cellular-connected unmanned aerial systems, a new switching mechanism is proposed in [\[316\]](#page-59-16), this novel switching mechanism ensures robust wireless connectivity and mobility support for drone terminals. Dynamic optimization is performed through reinforcement learning, and experimental results demonstrate that the dynamic optimization method can significantly reduce the number of switching times and ensure effective connections. Different from [\[315\]](#page-59-15), the authors in [\[317\]](#page-59-17) investigate the mobility management issue in UDNs through combining task offloading process. Due to the multiple options available for providing access and computing services to users in UDNs, mobility management becomes more difficult. Therefore, the authors propose an online optimization scheme based on Q-learning. Firstly, the optimal switching decisions can be made by predicting upcoming information. Secondly, through using the switching cost of offloading tasks as a penalty, the offloading delay can be minimized. Finally, a large number of experimental results indicate that the proposed switching scheme can effectively reduce the average latency and switching rate of user computing tasks.

<span id="page-31-7"></span><span id="page-31-6"></span><span id="page-31-2"></span>In [\[318\]](#page-59-18), the authors propose a novel dynamic programming-based mobility management scheme to minimize the average delay under the energy consumption constraint, then a cooperative transmission mechanism is adopted to reduce the transmission delay. Furthermore, they propose a mobility management scheme based on DQN algorithm. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed mobility management scheme can obtain optimal <span id="page-32-1"></span>delay performance and reduce the frequency of handover. In dense wireless local area networks (WLAN), traditional switching mechanisms can cause significant delays during the switching process. To address the issues of when to trigger switching and which access point to connect to in WLAN, the authors in [\[319\]](#page-59-19) first design an SDN based WLAN framework. Secondly, a handover management scheme based on DQN is proposed, which can learn the actual user behavior and network state based on the time-varying WLAN environment. In addition, the authors also introduce CNN and RNN to extract fine-grained discriminative features during switching. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed switching scheme can effectively improve data rate. In [\[320\]](#page-59-20), the authors propose a handover management scheme for millimeter wave communication under the heterogeneous network. In order to solve the significant fluctuations of the signals, they propose a DQN-based handover scheme which takes the users' connectivity and throughput into account, the simulation results reveal that the proposed algorithm can reduce the handover rate up to 70% and can achieve higher throughput compared to other solutions.

<span id="page-32-3"></span><span id="page-32-2"></span>In IoT scenarios, due to the limited size of sensor nodes and the difficulty of charging or replacing batteries of sensors, multi-hop data transmission often has high latency and imbalance business load. The authors in [\[321\]](#page-59-21) propose a UAV assisted IoT architecture that utilizes UAVs to achieve low latency, seamless coverage during sensor data collection process. Secondly, a decision layer, management layer, and aggregation layer are introduced into the architecture to handle machine learning, UAV management, and data aggregation issues, respectively. Considering that different sensor nodes have different sensitivity to delay, the author proposes a realtime path planning scheme based on DDQN. Finally, a grid based method is adopted to reduce the complexity of the scheme. The experimental results show that this scheme can effectively improve network performance. The authors in [\[322\]](#page-59-22) consider the network switching problem caused by dense base station deployment in millimeter wave scenarios. The increase in the number of switches has led to a decrease in QoS and QoE, as well as higher signaling overhead. Therefore, in this article, the authors propose a DDQN algorithm to reduce switching frequency in millimeter wave networks. In addition, this article adopts offline learning algorithm to reduce the computational costs caused by online learning. The simulation results show that the proposed scheme can effectively improve the QoS of users. End-to-end network slicing has become a key technology in 5G networks. It can enable traditional networks to support diversified businesses in vertical industries. Therefore, the author in [\[323\]](#page-59-23) proposes a heterogeneous cellular network system that supports network slicing function. In this system, dynamically switching network slices is adopted to improve system performance. Specifically, the author first models the end-to-end network switching problem as an MDP based on the delay requirements of different tasks to maximize user service profits and reduce switching costs. Then, a network slicing switching algorithm based on DDQN is proposed to train the intelligent agent. Finally, the simulation <span id="page-32-6"></span>results indicate that the algorithm has better performance compared to the baseline scheme.

<span id="page-32-7"></span>Different from the above work, the authors in [\[324\]](#page-59-24) consider a blockchain based mobility management framework in the ultra dense edge computing scenario. Under this framework, not only the continuity of mobile users' computing business can be guaranteed, but also the security of offloaded tasks can be guaranteed. Specifically, the first step is to utilize distributed ledgers to reduce duplicate verification issues between edge servers. In addition, blockchain storage of communication keys ensures communication security between base stations. Secondly, the wireless handover and service migration between base stations are modeled as multi-objective optimization problems, and then the Lyapunov optimization method is used to transform this problem into a long-term delay minimization problem. Finally, a DRL algorithm based on the actor critic framework is used to learn the optimal wireless switching strategy. The experimental results show that the proposed scheme can ensure the quality of service for users while reducing the number of switching between base stations. In [\[325\]](#page-59-25), in order to tackle the problem of dynamic handover between edge servers in mobile edge computing, the authors propose a handover optimization in MEC by DRL and they construct an adaptive weight DDPG algorithm to optimize the migration cost and service latency. The authors in [\[326\]](#page-59-26) consider a trajectory prediction problem for UAVs in a dynamic environment, such as moving nodes, exiting existing nodes, and adding emergency nodes. Specifically, a two-level DRL framework is proposed. In the first level, the framework models a dynamically changing environment. In the second layer, a DQN algorithm is used to perform trajectory planning based on the environmental features obtained from the first layer. Furthermore, due to the fact that in practical scenarios, UAVs may not know all the information about the environment, the author models the trajectory planning problem as a POMDP problem and uses DRL based algorithms to solve the optimal trajectory prediction problem. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the algorithm has a higher success rate in data collection.

## <span id="page-32-8"></span><span id="page-32-4"></span><span id="page-32-0"></span>*D. Lesson Learned and Future Directions*

The fifth-generation mobile communication technology has spawned many emerging application scenarios that expect high throughput and low latency. Many researchers have extensively investigated the mobility management issue in the CETCN, but there are still many challenges in optimizing mobility management issue using DRL. This section briefly discusses the existing challenges and possible future research directions.

- <span id="page-32-5"></span>• *Real-time Decision-making:* Mobility events require quick decision-making to ensure seamless connectivity. DRL algorithms must make decisions in real time to optimize handovers and resource allocation, which can be challenging due to the computational demands of DRL training.
- *Network Heterogeneity:* Cloud-edge-terminal collaborative networks consist of heterogeneous resources with

varying capabilities and latency. Coordinating between different types of resources to ensure seamless mobility requires sophisticated algorithms.

- *Resource Allocation:* DRL algorithms need to decide how to allocate resources (e.g., computing, storage, bandwidth) between the cloud and edge to optimize mobility management.
- *Interference and Signal Quality:* DRL algorithms need to account for interference and signal quality variations when making mobility decisions. Poor handover decisions could lead to degraded service quality.
- *Edge Computing Constraints:* Edge resources might have constraints in terms of processing power, memory, and storage. DRL algorithms should consider these limitations when making mobility-related decisions.

## <span id="page-33-0"></span>VIII. APPLICATIONS FOR AI-ENABLED CLOUD-EDGE-TERMINAL COLLABORATIVE NETWORK

With the further development of intelligent transportation (ITS), industrial Internet of Things (IIoT), smart health and digital agriculture, the computing capacity of various terminal devices has also been further enhanced. Therefore, according to the different requirements of users on performance indicators, these tasks can be processed on the terminal, edge or cloud. In this section, we make a comprehensive investigation on task offloading, resource allocation, caching and mobility management in intelligent transportation systems, IIoT, smart health and digital agriculture in Sections [VIII-A](#page-33-1)[–VIII-D.](#page-45-0) We also point out several challenges and possible future directions in Section [VIII-E.](#page-46-0) Summaries of this section are shown in Tables [IX,](#page-34-0) [X,](#page-35-0) [XII,](#page-37-0) [XIII,](#page-38-0) [XI,](#page-36-0) [XIV](#page-38-1) and [XVI.](#page-40-0)

## <span id="page-33-1"></span>*A. Intelligent Transportation Systems*

Advanced communication technologies, IoT sensor networks, and modern decision theory make ITS increasingly efficient under CETCN. Hence, in this subsection, we summarize the tasks scheduling, resource allocation, and discuss the challenges and future directions.

In Fig. [10,](#page-33-2) we depict a typical vehicle Ad-hoc network. In this network, vehicles are equipped with various processing units that can provide computation, caching, and communication capacities for users. Road Side Units (RSUs) can provide more powerful capacities compared with vehicles and cloud servers are considered to provide the most powerful computing, caching, and communication capabilities. In the following, we summarize related topics under the ITS system.

<span id="page-33-3"></span>*1) Task Offloading and Resource Allocation:* In the vehicle network, due to the lack of continuous power grid connection for roadside units, their charging mainly relies on human intervention or energy collection technology. Therefore, in order to ensure the continuous operation of the network, it is crucial to make reasonable use of roadside unit energy before the next charging cycle arrives. Therefore, the author in [\[327\]](#page-59-27) proposes a Q-learning algorithm, with the aim of conducting reasonable electricity scheduling within a cycle of roadside unit electricity consumption, in order to maximize the satisfaction of service requests. Extensive experimental results

![](_page_33_Picture_12.jpeg)

Fig. 10. An introduction of intelligent transportation systems.

<span id="page-33-6"></span><span id="page-33-5"></span><span id="page-33-4"></span><span id="page-33-2"></span>indicate that the Q-learning based algorithm can increase the amount of served vehicles. Yan et al. [\[328\]](#page-59-28) propose an access technology that aggregates multiple wireless access technologies to maximize the long-term throughput of the network while meeting different QoS requirements. However, in order to cope with the complex and dynamic network environment, they formulate the access problem as a Markov model, and then solve the problem through using multi-agent reinforcement learning technology. In addition, the authors also introduce Nash equilibrium to obtain feasible solutions while reducing the search space of multi-agent systems. Finally, the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm has been verified through a large number of simulation experiments. The authors in [\[329\]](#page-59-29) propose an intelligent task offloading scheme based on DRL to address the high dynamism of vehicle networks and resource constraints of vehicles. Specifically, firstly, they establish an optimization problem for task offloading and resource allocation, taking into account energy consumption and latency. Secondly, due to the complexity of the established problem, the authors divide it into two subproblems. For the task offloading subproblem, a bilateral matching scheme is proposed to maximize utility. The authors propose a DQN based algorithm to solve resource allocation subproblem, and introduce dropout regularization to solve the overestimation problem of DQN. Finally, the simulation results show that the improved DQN algorithm outperforms the baseline scheme under different parameter settings. The authors in [\[330\]](#page-59-30) propose an intelligent offloading framework for 5G vehicle networks under the coexistence of multiple access technologies. Firstly, considering the performance needs of users for various applications, a task offloading problem with cost minimization is proposed. Secondly, due to the complexity of the problem, they decompose it into two sub problems, i.e., task offloading between vehicles and roadside units, and task offloading between vehicles and infrastructure. Then, they

propose a bidirectional matching algorithm to solve the first subproblem, and propose a distributed DQN algorithm to solve the second subproblem. Compared with the centralized DRL algorithm, the proposed algorithm can significantly reduce communication overhead. Finally, the effectiveness of the proposed solution in task offloading is demonstrated through a real-world car rental scenario. Khayyat et al. [\[331\]](#page-59-31) propose a computational offloading algorithm for multi-level vehicle edge networks based on DRL. Firstly, in order to reduce the energy consumption of vehicles and ensure the effective utilization of resources among multiple vehicles, the author

models the task offloading and resource allocation problem as a binary problem. Secondly, due to the problem is NPhard, they propose an intelligent algorithm based on DQN to find the approximate optimal solution of the problem. Finally, simulation results demonstrate that the proposed architecture can reduce the energy consumption compared to the baseline solutions.

dynamically coordinate computing tasks and content caching, and maximize the profits of network operators. Specifically, this profit function fully considers the revenue of mobile operators and the QoE level of users. Secondly, a mobility aware task offloading and resource allocation scheme based on DDPG algorithm is proposed for the mobile vehicles. Finally, extensive experimental results verify the scheme can maximize the profits of MNOs and ensure the users' QoS under the condition of real traffic data. In order to solve the problem of energy consumption and delay caused by wireless channel changes over time under the Internet of Vehicles (IoVs), the authors in [\[333\]](#page-59-33) propose a task offloading framework assisted by edge computing to improve the efficiency of task offloading. Firstly, they design a task offloading model for heterogeneous vehicle networks, which considers multiple random tasks and the variation of wireless channels over time. Secondly, in order to achieve a balance between energy consumption and processing delay, and avoid the curse of dimensionality caused by excessive action space, an adaptive computing offloading method based on DDPG algorithm is proposed. Finally, the simulation results show that the proposed method outperforms DDQN and greedy algorithms. In order to reduce the service delay of the vehicle network and improve the reliability of the service, the authors in [\[334\]](#page-59-34) design a MEC collaborative computing framework. Firstly, a task scheduling algorithm is proposed to determine the execution order of tasks on edge servers. Secondly, they formulate the task offloading problem as an MDP and propose a DDPG algorithm to determine the task offloading strategy for vehicles to reduce the cost of collaborative computing. Finally, simulation results show that the proposed DDPG-based collaborative computing scheme can better response to the change of the environment <span id="page-35-3"></span>compared to the baseline solutions. Unlike the above work, in [\[335\]](#page-59-35), the authors propose a joint allocation problem of communication, computing, and storage resources in MEC based vehicle networks. Firstly, for different IoVs applications, the authors consider two typical MEC scenarios of placing MEC servers on macro stations and edge nodes, and construct corresponding multidimensional resource optimization frameworks. Secondly, due to the high computational complexity and solution time of the problem, they develop a DDPG based algorithm to solve the optimization problem. Through offline training, this algorithm can quickly obtain the optimal allocation strategy to meet the QoS requirements of different applications. The experimental results show that the algorithm can achieve high QoS satisfaction.

<span id="page-35-5"></span><span id="page-35-4"></span><span id="page-35-2"></span><span id="page-35-1"></span>In order to solve the problem of long task processing time caused by a large number of offloading tasks, the authors in [\[336\]](#page-59-36) propose a distributed task offloading method based on transformer and policy decoupling. Firstly, the task offloading problem in the vehicle network is modelled as a mixed cooperation competition multi-agent behavior coordination problem, with the aim of selecting the optimal offloading node. Secondly, an improved MADDPG algorithm is proposed to solve the optimization problem. Specifically, a long sequence prediction network is introduced in this algorithm to predict the future queuing delay of the network, the actor network is redesigned using a transformer based time feature extraction network (TTFEN) and a policy decoupling network. Due to the use of TTFEN, this algorithm can adapt to any size of input. Finally, training on real datasets shows that the proposed algorithm can not only quickly adapt to new vehicle network environments, but can also effectively reduce costs. The authors in [\[337\]](#page-59-37) consider a novel remote intelligent transportation system, which is mainly aimed at various applications of

aircraft and ships. Firstly, due to the insufficient ground access network to provide flexible coverage for these applications, the author proposes using low orbit satellites to provide computing offloading services for aircraft and ships. Secondly, in order to optimize the resource allocation of different satellite servers, they propose an optimization problem of joint delay and price. Due to the fact that each satellite intelligently obtains local observation data, and at the same time, the decision of a single satellite can affect global optimality, the author describes the problem as a POMDP with global excitation. To solve this problem, they design a MADRL algorithm with attention mechanism to obtain the optimal offloading decision. Finally, a large number of simulation results have proven the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm. Unlike the above work, the authors in [\[338\]](#page-59-38) propose a vehicle to vehicle (V2V) distributed dynamic task offloading framework, taking the personal service and offloading intentions of private vehicles into account, to improve the utilization of edge resources. Firstly, in order to collaborate in a more effective manner among vehicles, a stable cooperative relationship between vehicles is established using alliance games and pricing mechanisms. Secondly, in order to achieve stable and efficient offloading services and fully tap into the potential of local computing capability, they describe the transaction process as a POMDP. At the same time, a multi-agent based actor critic algorithm is designed to obtain the optimal offloading strategy. Finally, the experimental results show that the proposed algorithm outperforms the baseline scheme

<span id="page-36-2"></span>in dynamic environments. In [\[339\]](#page-59-39), the authors investigate the dynamic distributed computing offloading problem in heterogeneous road networks, where multiple heterogeneous computing entities simultaneously provide computing services to vehicles. Firstly, considering the autonomous offloading mode of vehicles, they model task offloading and resource allocation problem into a distributed Stackelberg game, where MEC servers responsible for allocating resources and vehicles responsible for offloading tasks. Secondly, due to the information obtained by each vehicle is often incomplete, the authors design an optimal strategy search scheme based on partially observable Markov processes to maximize long-term returns. On this basis, an MADDPG algorithm is proposed to learn the optimal task offloading and resource allocation scheme. Finally, numerical results indicate that the proposed algorithm is superior than other baseline solutions in terms of response tine.

<span id="page-36-3"></span><span id="page-36-1"></span>*2) Caching:* The author in [\[340\]](#page-59-40) proposes a resource allocation strategy that combines communication, computation, and caching to improve the cost efficiency of vehicle networks. Firstly, the author proposes a mobile aware encoding caching and computing offloading scheme, where computing tasks can only be offloaded to roadside units when the vehicle can obtain content from them. Otherwise, the content required by the vehicle and the services needed to be offloaded must be completed by the base station. Then, considering the limited storage capacity and computing power of roadside units, as well as the end-to-end delay of services required

by vehicles, a resource allocation problem of minimizing cost for joint caching and offloading is formulated. At the same time, in order to reduce the complexity of the problem caused by the large action space, they develop a dual time scale optimization algorithm to configure the parameters of cache and computing resources. Specifically, large time scales use particle swarm optimization method to allocate cache, while small time scales use DQN algorithm for computing resource optimization. Finally, numerical results show that the proposed algorithm can achieve significant performance gain compared with other baseline solutions. The authors in [\[341\]](#page-59-41) propose a collaborative caching algorithm for vehicle networks based on federated reinforcement learning to address the content replacement issue, and thus adapt the high dynamics of vehicle networks and rapid changes in content popularity. Firstly, each vehicle collects data separately and trains using its own computing unit. Then, each vehicle uploads the trained parameters to the roadside unit and the roadside unit aggregates the collected parameters. After aggregation, all roadside units upload the parameters to the base station for global aggregation. Finally, the base station sends globally aggregated parameters to each vehicle for training. In this manner, the computational and communication costs of vehicles can be greatly reduced. In addition, in order to meet the cache capacity constraint, the author proposes a DQN based <span id="page-37-2"></span>algorithm to maximize the cache hit rate. Simulation results show that the proposed algorithm can improve cache hit rate while accelerating convergence speed. In [\[342\]](#page-59-42), the author considers a vehicle node assisted content delivery network and proposes a content migration strategy, the proposed strategy can help users obtain the required content from the edge network easily. However, in order to fully utilize the cache resources of edge networks and determine which edge server is used to offload content, they propose a DQN based algorithm to minimize costs and ensure the reasonable utilization of edge resources. Secondly, this article not only considers the popularity of content, but also considers the impact of content priority on cost. At the same time, in order to adapt to the dynamic nature of the vehicle network, the author also adds a LSTM network to the DQN network to automatically learn and update its parameters. Finally, simulation results indicate that the DQN-based approach can reduce 70% content access delay compared to the traditional methods.

macro base stations, roadside units and intelligent vehicles to jointly optimize the content placement of vehicle edge computing networks. They describe the optimization problem as an MDP with dual time scales. Firstly, compared to the mobility of vehicles and changes in the network, the frequency of content popularity changes is relatively low. Therefore, on a large time scale, content placement strategies are determined based on the popularity of content, the trajectory of vehicles, and the availability of resources. Secondly, on a small time scale, a bandwidth allocation scheme is designed to meet the latency of content acquisition, with the aim of minimizing the cost of accessing content. As this problem is an MINLP problem, they propose a DDPG algorithm to obtain suboptimal solutions with lower computational complexity. Finally, extensive experimental results indicate the proposed scheme can reduce the system cost and content delivery time compared to the other caching schemes. In [\[345\]](#page-60-2), the authors propose a hierarchical terminal-edge collaborative vehicle network caching framework to minimize the network overhead. Then, a DDPG based near-optimal solution is proposed to solve the problem. Finally, the performance analysis indicates that the framework outperforms other baseline solutions at reducing the network overhead.

<span id="page-39-1"></span><span id="page-39-0"></span>*3) Mobility Management:* Mobile edge computing (MEC) based heterogeneous vehicular networks (HetVNets) are attractive technologies to support low-latency applications in vehicles. However, in practical systems, joint vehicle-toedge (V2E) association and resource allocation is an urgent problem, since precise prior knowledge of the dynamic wireless environment is difficult to obtain. Therefore, in [\[346\]](#page-60-3), the author first uses random geometry to simulate real <span id="page-39-3"></span><span id="page-39-2"></span>scenarios. Specifically, the intersection is first set up as two vertical streets, and then the distribution of vehicles on the streets is modeled as different independent poisson point processes. Secondly, considering the delay for different types of vehicles to receive services, they propose a DDQN algorithm to solve the connection problem between vehicles and edge networks. On the one hand, this algorithm considers the transmission delay, computational delay, and switching cost. On the other hand, it can minimize service time while meeting computational resource constraints. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm has a shorter service time than the baseline scheme. Unlike the above work, the authors in [\[347\]](#page-60-4) consider a practical deployment scenario to validate the algorithm for handover management. Firstly, based on configurable input parameters in cellular networks, a DDQN algorithm is proposed for handover management, and the performance of the proposed algorithm is evaluated using the network simulator ns3 and LTE module. At the same time, a real network is set up in the city center of Glasgow, and vehicle trajectories generated by a route movement model and Google Maps are configured for algorithm performance evaluation. The simulation results and real network validation show that the proposed algorithm outperforms the baseline scheme in terms of packet loss rate and service quality. A user centered cluster(UC) enhances the connectivity of mobile users by grouping an appropriate number of access points. However, for high-speed vehicles, UC clustering may lead to excessively frequent switching, thereby reducing throughput and increasing energy consumption. In order to solve the problem caused by excessive switching, the author in [\[348\]](#page-60-5) proposes a new cluster migration strategy, which can perform horizontal and

vertical switching between V2V and V2I. Specifically, in order to achieve a balance between connectivity and switching times, the author formulates an optimal switching problem under the minimum data rate and association constraints. Secondly, a DDPG algorithm is proposed to determine the migration scheme for UC clusters. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme can achieve higher data rates than the baseline scheme. Khan et al. [\[349\]](#page-60-6) investigate the correlation problem between vehicles and cells in millimeter wave communication scenarios, with the aim of maximizing the average speed of vehicles while ensuring relatively low vehicle overhead. To address the above issues, the author first formulates an optimization problem that maximizes the average speed of all vehicles while minimizing the probability of the average speed falling below a certain threshold. Secondly, an actor critic based reinforcement learning algorithm is proposed to obtain the approximate optimal solution. In the proposed algorithm, each roadside unit makes independent decisions based on its own observations and utilizes offline training schemes to reduce computational load at the same time. Finally, the simulation results show that the proposed scheme can reduce the interruption probability while meeting the constraint of average rate.

<span id="page-40-4"></span><span id="page-40-3"></span>Making service migration decisions based on complete system information to ensure user service quality not only incurs extremely high latency but also lacks scalability. Therefore, in [\[350\]](#page-60-7), the author models the service migration problem as a POMDP, which does not require all information about the system to make effective migration decisions. At the same time, a DRL based offline actor critic algorithm is used to make migration decisions and improve the stability of training. Secondly, the author introduces the LSTM network to effectively extract hidden information from historical data. Simulation results show that the proposed method outperforms other baseline schemes. Mobile vehicles cannot obtain continuous services by accessing a fixed MEC server. Therefore, the authors in [\[351\]](#page-60-8) propose a service migration problem in a V2X scenario in this paper. By modeling the service migration problem as a Markov process, and comprehensively considering computational costs, migration costs, and energy consumption of vehicles during movement. Second, an actorcritic based deep reinforcement learning algorithm is proposed to solve the Markov process. Finally, simulation results show that the proposed algorithm is more robust.
