## <span id="page-40-1"></span>*B. Industrial IoT*

Normally, there are a huge number of devices with limited computing and storage resources in an IIoT scenario, and these devices generate a large amount of data that need to be processed. For example, as shown in Fig. [11,](#page-40-1) typical IIoT scenarios include smart grid, smart manufacturing and so on, the processing devices in these scenarios have limited computing power, and tens of millions of data needs to be processed will put a huge burden on these systems.

<span id="page-40-5"></span>The complex and real time decision-making process, dynamic environment of IIoT and heterogeneous capacity of devices are the main challenges of IIoT system. For example, the authors in [\[352\]](#page-60-9) propose a joint task offloading and resource allocation problem. Through considering the instability caused by multihop transmission, a non-convex optimization problem is established to minimize task processing delay, and convex optimization methods are used to solve the problem. However, constructing convex optimization problems requires additional computational and communication costs, which can become a burden in resource constrained IIoT devices. In addition, convex optimization methods require a <span id="page-41-2"></span><span id="page-41-1"></span>long time to solve large-scale task offloading and resource allocation problems with many devices and tasks [\[353\]](#page-60-10). The author in [\[354\]](#page-60-11) proposes a computational offloading strategy based on potential game to minimize the computational time and energy consumption of each task. This method solves the problem of focusing on single optimization objective in convex optimization [\[355\]](#page-60-12), and has good scalability with the increase of IIoT devices. However, this method requires the assumption that all players participating in the game are rational [\[356\]](#page-60-13), and in real-world IIoT scenarios, due to equipment failures and other issues, this assumption is not always reasonable. But fortunately, DRL can effectively overcome the problems brought by the traditional methods mentioned above. Therefore, DRL can become a promising method to handle task offloading, resource allocation, service placement and mobility management issues in IIoT environment.

<span id="page-41-3"></span>*1) Task Offloading and Resource Allocation:* Traditional computing offloading methods are not suitable for IIoT scenarios due to their high computational latency. In [\[357\]](#page-60-14), the author proposes a task offloading method based on DRL, which minimizes long-term offloading energy consumption in scenarios with multiple IIoT nodes and access points. Specifically, through creating DRL agent for each IIoT node, the action space can be reduced. Secondly, after selecting access points, each access point executes a low complexity greedy algorithm to determine which tasks should be offloaded to the cloud for processing under computational power constraints. Through offline training in the cloud and online decision-making processes, access points can quickly adjust their selection of IIoT devices. Finally, the simulation results show that the proposed scheme is superior to the exhaustive method and genetic algorithm. The authors in [\[358\]](#page-60-15) consider a machine to machine assisted NOMA based computing offloading scheme, where IIoT devices can offload tasks to devices with idle computing resources through machine to machine communication. Specifically, they propose a DRL based sleep scheduling mechanism, which optimizes communication and computing resources through sleep mechanisms while satisfying task delay constraint and energy consumption constraint. Secondly, an iterative optimization algorithm is designed to solve the resource allocation problem. Finally, the numerical results indicate that the scheme can efficiently reduce the system's energy consumption and satisfy the computation requirements of users at the same time. Due to the fact that in the field of IIoT, computing tasks generated by various devices often have different service requirements. The distribution of these tasks has a basic internal pattern and varies depending on regional, seasonal, and temporal factors. Therefore, in [\[359\]](#page-60-16), the authors consider developing specific task offloading schemes for specific tasks to reduce task execution delays. In order to learn these generation patterns and improve task completion rate, the authors develop a DRL based task offloading algorithm. At the same time, a multi-layer computing architecture supporting SDN is introduced to efficiently allocate task features based on the obtained factors. Finally, the proposed algorithm can improve the task completion rate and achieve near-optimal solution in IIoT scenarios. Due to the dynamic and continuous nature of task completion in the IIoT scenarios, in [\[360\]](#page-60-17), the <span id="page-41-8"></span><span id="page-41-0"></span>authors investigate the dynamic resource management problem of joint power control and computing resource allocation. Firstly, total latency generated by all tasks is optimized in the process of dynamically generating tasks. Secondly, the authors transform the long-term delay minimization problem to an MDP. Considering the dynamic characteristics of task generation, a DDPG algorithm is proposed to obtain the optimal solution. Finally, through extensive simulations, the proposed method can reduce the long-term average delay of the tasks compared to other baseline solutions. Unlike the above work, in [\[361\]](#page-60-18), the authors propose an intelligent driven green resource allocation mechanism for IIoT in heterogeneous networks from the perspective of efficiency and energy conservation. Firstly, the authors propose an end-to-end selforganizing resource allocation framework for IIoT. Secondly, under the premise of meeting the quality of service, with the goal of maximizing energy efficiency, a green resource allocation model is constructed with the number of devices and base station resources as variables. Simultaneously, a DRL algorithm based on actor critic framework is used to solve the optimization problem. Simulation results indicate that the proposed method can achieve better performance at quality of service compared with other traditional methods.

<span id="page-41-9"></span><span id="page-41-7"></span><span id="page-41-6"></span><span id="page-41-5"></span><span id="page-41-4"></span>The authors in [\[148\]](#page-55-28) study the task offloading problem of IoT systems under rapidly time-varying wireless channel conditions. Unlike assumptions that consider IoT devices can obtain channel state information, due to hardware limitations, short sensor sensing time, and network connectivity issues, it is difficult for IoT devices to have a perfect understanding of dynamic channel conditions. Therefore, the authors propose a partially observable IoT task offloading scheme in this article, which enables IoT devices to make optimal offloading decisions under imperfect channel conditions. Secondly, the authors formulate the optimization problem as a POMDP that minimizes the energy consumption of IoT devices while meeting task processing delays. In order to find the optimal strategy, an offline algorithm based on deep recursive Qnetwork is proposed. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm can effectively reduce the energy consumption of IoT devices while ensuring computational latency. Unlike the previous work, the authors in [\[362\]](#page-60-19) not only consider the characteristics of wireless channels changing over time, but also consider the challenges of unknown dynamic IIoT environments such as the diversity of task requests, limited resources, and dynamic load changes on task offloading decisions and NOMA user pairing. Therefore, in order to address the aforementioned challenges, the authors first consider task offloading problem and NOMA subchannel allocation problem in a dynamic environment. This problem describes the effects of task transmission delay, task processing delay, and dynamic characteristics of the environment on user satisfaction. Secondly, due to the signaling overhead required for each edge server to obtain all information about IIoT devices is large, the authors consider a distributed DRL algorithm where each IIoT device interacts with the environment as an agent and makes the best decision. Due to this method transforming the original problem into a POMDP problem, they use recursive neural networks to predict the real-time <span id="page-42-0"></span>load of all subchannels and servers, and the agent use the predicted results to make decision. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme is superior than baseline schemes in terms of task satisfaction ratio. Since the IoT systems assisted by low orbit satellites can overcome terrain obstacles, they are suitable for computing offloading in scenarios such as sparsely populated rural areas and disaster sites. However, sharing communication and power resources with users and satellites is a challenging task. Therefore, the author in [\[363\]](#page-60-20) studies the task offloading problem in the LEO-IoT scenario, with the aim of making the fast offloading decisions and improving energy efficiency. Specifically, firstly, the author models the collaborative offloading problem as an optimization problem with resource constraints. Secondly, the author describes the problem as a POMDP problem and proposes an algorithm for multi-agent information broadcasting and judgment. In addition, the resource allocation decision is obtain through the collaboration among agents. Finally, extensive numerical results indicate that the proposed algorithm achieves a better performance in transmission latency and energy consumption than several baseline algorithms. The authors in [\[364\]](#page-60-21) propose a wireless resource allocation scheme in a controllable IoT scenario to achieve strict requirements for service quality in the uRLLC scenario. Firstly, they consider a cellular heterogeneous network and develop a dynamic mixed nonlinear integer programming resource allocation problem to maximize long-term average energy consumption while meeting reliability, latency, and transmission rate constraints. Secondly, a distributed MADRL resource allocation algorithm is proposed, while using Dec-POMDP to model some observable characteristics of agents in large-scale networks, where each agent does not require all information about the environment. Finally, large amount of results show that the proposed approach can achieve a better performance in maximizing energy efficiency than other traditional methods. The authors in [\[365\]](#page-60-22) investigate the distributed resource allocation problem of energy-saving data forwarding in resource constrained IIoT scenarios. Firstly, the authors formulate the problem as a Dec-POMDP based decision problem and propose an intelligent resource allocation algorithm based on a dual attention mechanism. Due to the introduction of a dual attention mechanism, it overcomes the difficulties of feature extraction in partially observable environments and non-stationary network environments. In addition, the multiscale convolutional attention mechanism is used for local state feature extraction, while the multi-head attention mechanism is used to extract global state information. Numerical results show the proposed scheme outperforms other baseline solutions in network lifetime, transmission reliability, and network stability. Tang et al. [\[366\]](#page-60-23) study the distributed task scheduling problem of IoT in serverless edge computing. Due to heterogeneous edge servers are rational individuals, the edge servers will maximize their own utility by observing local data considering the security issues. Secondly, since there is no complete information about the environment, the authors model the task scheduling problem as a stochastic game, in which each serverless edge computing node schedules the tasks and allocates resources in a non-cooperative manner according to the available computing resources and task queue status, in order to maximize its own utility while reducing delay and energy consumption. Then, in order to solve the POSG problem, the authors propose an intelligent algorithm based on DDQN to obtain the approximate optimal solution of the problem. Finally, simulation experiments show that the proposed algorithm can achieve effective task scheduling decisions with lower complexity.

The further development of the IIoT also requires edge servers to have caching capabilities. Unlike the content caching described in the previous chapters, caching in the IIoT scenario pays more attention to service placement, to provide vertical industries with the services they need nearby. Therefore, this section summarizes the DRL-based service placement problem in the IIoT scenarios.

<span id="page-42-7"></span><span id="page-42-6"></span><span id="page-42-5"></span><span id="page-42-4"></span><span id="page-42-3"></span><span id="page-42-2"></span><span id="page-42-1"></span>*2) Service Placement:* In [\[367\]](#page-60-24), in order to support the reliable operation in IIoT, the authors propose an NFVenabled cloud-edge collaborative architecture to provide flexible services for IIoT in the form of the service function chain (SFC). However, it is difficult to provide IIoT services with high qualities due to the limited caching capacity of edge servers. Hence, in order to handle the problem, a DQNbased method is presented to solve the SFC placement problem and hence provide reliable services to IIoT applications. Simulation results indicate that the proposed method can better balance the resource consumption and perform well in providing delay-sensitive IIoT services compared to other baseline solutions. Hazra et al. in [\[368\]](#page-60-25) design an intelligent service deployment strategy to simultaneously handle dynamic requests and edge resources allocation in IIoT networks. Firstly, the authors describe the problem as the sum of weighted energy delays under QoS constraints, and formulate the service deployment problem as an MINLP. Then, a heuristic task execution strategy is designed and a DRL algorithm is used to intelligently process the generated IIoT requests at the network edge. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm can achieve effective service deployment strategies. The dynamic arrival of task requests and limited edge network resources in the IIoT make service scheduling problems become a challenging issue. Therefore, the authors in [\[369\]](#page-60-26) introduces a service function chain (SFC) orchestration framework based on NFV technology to overcome the expensive cost of applying and expanding traditional networks. Specifically, first of all, considering timeliness and resource availability, the authors describe the problem as an utility maximization problem, and divide it into SFC request selection and SFC dynamic scheduling problems. Secondly, for the first problem, a dynamic pricing mechanism and matching algorithm are proposed to select the current SFC request that should be executed. For the second problem, the DDQN algorithm is used to approximate the optimal cost. Finally, the numerical results show that the proposes method is more efficient than other baseline solutions. On the basis of the [\[369\]](#page-60-26), the authors in [\[370\]](#page-60-27) consider the deployment of SFC for wireless sensing scenario. Firstly, the authors describe a formulaic expression of wireless sensing deployment problem to minimize the service latency, the expression can not only control the traffic passing through wired links, but also control <span id="page-43-0"></span>the traffic passing through wireless links. Then, the allocation of wireless resources in the network and resources on IIoT servers are modeled as an MDP, and a DRL algorithm based on the actor critical framework is proposed to find the approximate optimal strategy. Secondly, in order to solve the high-dimensional problem of the action space, an optimization sub-algorithm based on norm penalty is introduced in the calculation of the reward function, thereby reducing the size of the action space and improving the convergence speed. Finally, numerical results indicate that the proposed method superior to other solutions. In [\[371\]](#page-60-28), the authors provide a method for virtual network function (VNF) placement with routing issues. Firstly, the VNF placement and routing problem is formulated to minimize service latency and resource consumption costs, and then decompose the problem into task placement subproblem and routing subproblem. Then a new MADRL framework is proposed to solve these two sub problems, which consists of a VNF placement module and a routing module. At the same time, the DDPG algorithm is introduced to find the optimal solution for these two modules. Finally, extensive experiments show that the proposed method superior than others in terms of service cost and delay, and can offer higher flexibility for personalized services. The authors in [\[372\]](#page-60-29) propose a dual time scale adaptive request scheduling and collaborative service caching joint optimization problem. Firstly, considering the limited computing and caching resources on MEC, the authors model the problem as a problem of maximizing the service latency reduction rate. Meanwhile, due to the fact that the time scale of content popularity changes is often greater than the time scale of request scheduling, they separate the service caching problem from the joint optimization problem. Then, in order to adapt to the unavailability of information, they formulate the service caching problem and request scheduling problem as a POMDP, and design a DRL algorithm to solve the approximate optimal solution. Furthermore, the Lagrangian multiplier method is used to allocate computing resources, thereby further minimizing the average service delay. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm can serve more requests while reduce service latency.

<span id="page-43-3"></span>*3) Mobility Management:* Since emerging mobile IoT services require extremely high transmission bandwidth and extremely low transmission latency, the authors in [\[373\]](#page-60-30) investigate the problem of user access control in mmWaveenabled edge computing systems. They consider the impact of system throughput and switching efficiency on the optimal extension range at the same time, and proposes a DQN algorithm to optimize the optimal extension range. The authors in [\[374\]](#page-60-31) consider a multi-user wireless mobile edge computing system scenario, where users can transmit tasks to edge base stations for processing through wireless channels. The user's task offloading decision can be adjusted according to the timevarying network environment. Since there are a large number of users and edge servers in the actual network, the author proposes a server selection, task offloading and switching strategy based on deep reinforcement learning. The simulation results show that the proposed algorithm can achieve better performance than traditional methods. In addition, the ultradense network is also considered as a key enabling technology

<span id="page-43-5"></span><span id="page-43-4"></span>in the fifth generation mobile communication. Ultra dense networks not only bring high data transmission but also problems of high interference and complex switching. In order to achieve seamless connection of base stations and provide high-quality services for users, in [\[375\]](#page-60-32), the author proposes an efficient handover algorithm based on DDPG. Specifically, this algorithm accurately determines the switching trigger point by dynamically adjusting key switching parameters such as switching margin. The simulation results show that the proposed algorithm improves switching performance while ensuring high throughput and low latency at different speed of vehicles. Due to the susceptibility of millimeter wave links to user and terrain influences, the author proposes a switching scheme based on jumping Markov linear systems and DRL in [\[376\]](#page-60-33) to achieve connectivity between devices and base stations and avoid unnecessary switching at the same time. In order to reduce training samples, the author introduces meta learning technology, which is a technology that makes new decisions by automatically reusing data from past or related tasks. Finally, the simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme can select more reliable links and reduce the number of handovers. The articles mentioned above mostly focus on improving user service quality through switching optimization. However, the author proposes an optimal switching scheme that ensures security while improving service quality in [\[377\]](#page-60-34). Specifically, firstly, a blockchain based mobility management scheme is proposed, which not only achieves user mobility management but also reduces the number of switches. Secondly, in order to improve the security of task offloading process, the author establishes a security model that includes confidentiality, integrity, and availability standards. Then, based on this standard, a differential evolution algorithm is designed that can ensure security and reduce costs. Finally, an intelligent algorithm based on DRL is proposed to solve the adaptive switching scheme in MEC environment, which can improve QoS while reducing switching risk.

<span id="page-43-8"></span><span id="page-43-7"></span><span id="page-43-6"></span><span id="page-43-2"></span><span id="page-43-1"></span>The scarcity of wireless spectrum resources leads to fierce competition among users. Therefore, in [\[378\]](#page-60-35), the authors propose an efficient dynamic spectrum access technique to ensure a fair allocation of available network resources. Then, aim to provide users with the most suitable spectrum access, a DRL based approach is proposed to evaluate channel occupancy and determine channel quality. The results show that the proposed method can significantly reduce communication latency and improve system throughput compared to traditional methods. Unlike the previous references on reducing the number of handovers to improve service quality, the authors in [\[379\]](#page-60-36) propose an active edge correlation technique that can improve wireless connectivity by increasing the frequency and energy consumption of handovers. Due to the dependence of this method on a large amount of private information sharing, this paper investigates the joint edge association problem in environmental uncertainty and privacy protection. Firstly, due to the uncertainty of the environment, the author models connectivity, switching, and long-term energy consumption as a Dec-POMDP problem. Secondly, they propose a multiagent federated reinforcement learning algorithm that shares encrypted local Q values. Specifically, when some vehicles

![](_page_44_Figure_2.jpeg)

<span id="page-44-0"></span>Fig. 12. CETCN Framework for Smart Health.

cannot learn independently, distributed decisions can be made based on the results of federated training. Simulation results show that the proposed solution improves user privacy compared with other baseline solutions.

## *C. Smart Health*

Smart health has become one of the important directions in today's social development as shown in Fig. [12.](#page-44-0) The growth of medical data and the heterogeneity of data have become the main challenges of smart medicine, especially in processing tasks and storing data, which makes it necessary to use edge computing and AI to make smart health more efficient. To be specific, DRL can optimize scheduling and coordination of healthcare operations, such as appointment scheduling, patient flow in hospitals. At the same time, in a smart health ecosystem, DRL can be used to dynamically allocate medical resources, such as hospital beds, surgical facilities, and medical staff, to ensure efficient and effective patient care. Next, we will introduce how to use DRL technology to solve the challenges of task offloading and resource allocation issue in smart healthcare. More relevant issues and challenges can be found in [\[380\]](#page-60-37), [\[381\]](#page-60-38) for interested readers.

<span id="page-44-3"></span><span id="page-44-2"></span><span id="page-44-1"></span>The detection of early symptoms in heart disease patients has always been a challenge for healthcare providers, and at the same time, traditional machine learning models are no longer able to meet the development of medical big data. Therefore, the author in [\[382\]](#page-60-39) proposes a computing paradigm based on cloud fog collaboration to provide low latency and energy-saving computing for healthcare systems. Simultaneously, the DQN algorithm is used to construct a heart disease prediction model. Finally, validation on a heart disease dataset shows that the proposed algorithm can improve prediction accuracy, reduce energy consumption and latency. The authors in [\[383\]](#page-60-40) develop a nested word remote procedure call based on reinforcement learning and federated <span id="page-44-4"></span>learning, with the aim of designing a consumer centric semi robot efficient training and testing system to execute the entire application with minimal latency in the medical IoT. Compared with traditional machine learning algorithms, this algorithm can effectively reduce the local processing time of tasks. The authors in [\[384\]](#page-60-41) propose a computing framework for ultra high reliability and extremely low latency healthcare applications in 6G networks. Firstly, they combine the wireless body area network (WBAN) and edge computing to enable IoMT to sense, process and transmit large amounts of data. Secondly, due to the excessive control frames generated during transmission process in WBAN, it leads to high latency and high energy consumption. Therefore, they propose a frame aggregation and task offloading method based on DQN. The algorithm divides different services based on QoS, and then the algorithm dynamically selects the frame aggregation length and the number of offloading tasks according to the requirements of latency and energy consumption. The simulation results show that the proposed algorithm can effectively reduce latency and energy consumption, and improve the throughput of WBAN. In healthcare systems, the freshness of data is crucial for monitoring the vital signs of patients. Therefore, the author in [\[385\]](#page-61-0) considers a resource management solution in an intelligent healthcare system. Specifically, edge nodes process data from wearable medical devices and then send the processed data to the medical center. The goal of edge nodes is to find the optimal resource management strategy that maximizes information freshness. Firstly, the author models the problem as a Markov process, and then designs an optimal strategy for minimizing system energy consumption and information freshness using DDQN algorithm. At the same time, the author also proposes a sleep mechanism to improve the energy efficiency of each wearable device. The simulation results show that the proposed scheme effectively balances energy consumption and information freshness.

<span id="page-44-7"></span><span id="page-44-6"></span><span id="page-44-5"></span>The wireless virtual reality medical treatment (WVMT) system that combines virtual reality technology with the medical IoT is a promising application in the future medical industry. However, due to the high requirements of medical services, improving the efficiency of the WVMT system is an urgent problem need to be solved. Therefore, in [\[386\]](#page-61-1), the author first proposes a blockchain supported task offloading problem. Then, the problem is described as a Markov decision problem with block consistency, content correlation, and channel fluctuation constraints. Finally, a quantum inspired actor critic algorithm is used to adaptively allocate resources according to the requirements of content transmission. The simulation results show that this algorithm can improve convergence speed and reduce energy consumption. The Health IoT has become a promising electronic health approach by uploading a large number of computationally intensive services to edge servers. Considering that current computing offloading technologies are unable to meet the growing health needs of people in ultra reliable and low latency communication environment, the authors in [\[387\]](#page-61-2) propose a multi-agent based task offloading and resource allocation scheme that can maximize throughput while minimizing terminal power consumption. Firstly, the authors use Lyapunov

optimization method to decouple the problem into task offloading subproblem and resource allocation subproblem. Secondly, the discrete multi-agent SAC algorithm and queue squeezing perception algorithm are used to solve two sub problems, respectively. Finally, a large number of simulation results indicate that the proposed algorithm outperforms traditional DRL algorithms in terms of data arrival rate and latency. Unlike the above work, the author in [\[388\]](#page-61-3) considers the real-time monitoring problem in the medical IoT. Due to the additional energy consumption will be generated by offloading services from medical monitoring units to edge servers, a service offloading problem with A3C algorithm is proposed to optimize energy perception. This algorithm can dynamically adjust delay energy correction based on business class and system requirements. Finally, the effectiveness of the algorithm is verified through actual data. The author in [\[389\]](#page-61-4) proposes a healthcare data offloading scheme based on blockchain technology, which can achieve safe and efficient data offloading. Specifically, the first step is to introduce a smart contract mechanism for security and integrity supervision of user data. Secondly, an optimization problem integrating offloading decisions, resource allocation, and blockchain data audit costs is proposed. Finally, a DRL algorithm is used to obtain the optimal solution of the problem. The simulation results show that the proposed scheme can ensure the security of user data while reducing costs. The author in [\[390\]](#page-61-5) considers UAV assisted medical IoT systems in remote network coverage or edge server overload/emergency situations. Firstly, in order to ensure the security of user data and minimize system costs while considering latency and energy consumption, the author proposes a multi-agent federated reinforcement learning framework. In this framework, medical IoT devices train their own models and send parameters to UAVs to aggregate the global model. Secondly, the author proposes a resource optimization problem for joint task offloading and information age, and transforms this problem into an MDP. Finally, a multiagent reinforcement learning algorithm based on MADDPG is developed to solve the optimization problem. Last but not least, the proposed algorithm combines federated learning models to obtain the optimal solution while ensuring user data security.

## <span id="page-45-0"></span>*D. Digital Agriculture*

Smart agriculture has become a key solution for the sustainable development of agriculture and solving the global food crisis. Machine learning and deep learning are crucial for the development of smart agriculture. Machine learning algorithms can be used for crop selection and management, and deep learning can be used for crop yield prediction. In these applications, a large amount of data is sent through terminal devices to edge servers and cloud servers for processing as shown in Fig. [13.](#page-45-1) In order to better utilize the resources of edge devices, reasonable task offloading and resource allocation schemes should be developed. Therefore, this section will conduct a survey on the task offloading and resource allocation issues designed in smart agriculture. Interested readers can

<span id="page-45-2"></span>![](_page_45_Figure_5.jpeg)

<span id="page-45-3"></span><span id="page-45-1"></span>Fig. 13. CETCN Framework for Digital Agriculture.

<span id="page-45-5"></span><span id="page-45-4"></span>refer to [\[391\]](#page-61-6), [\[392\]](#page-61-7) on other issues and challenges in smart agriculture.

<span id="page-45-7"></span><span id="page-45-6"></span>The deep learning architecture with IoT, UAVs, and edge clouds has made smart agriculture become an important component of the agricultural revolution. In [\[393\]](#page-61-8), the author proposes a two-stage end-to-end DRL smart agriculture system. Specifically, in the first stage, a DQN model based on ant colony algorithm is proposed to complete task offloading issues such as fire detection, pest detection, crop growth, and irrigation scheduling. This model offloads tasks to UAVs, edge clouds, or core clouds based on energy consumption, latency, and computing power. In the second stage, the task of predicting and monitoring various agricultural activities is carried out through using DQN algorithm. A large number of simulation experiments show that the proposed scheme can improve the accuracy and precision of smart agricultural systems. Due to advanced carrier aggregation (CA) technology and MEC technology, 5G and 6G networks can be used to support resource and computationally intensive services of smart agriculture. CA technology achieves higher peak rates by aggregating multiple frequency bands, while MEC reduces latency and costs by offloading tasks. Therefore, in [\[394\]](#page-61-9), the author studies the use of CA and MEC technologies in 5G and 6G networks to solve the task offloading problem of computationally intensive services in smart agriculture. Firstly, the author proposes a reinforcement learning based sequential decision algorithm to solve the offloading problem, with the aim of achieving a balance between maximum transmission rate and minimum transmission power. In addition, the author also maximizes network capacity and improves network coverage by activating/disabling composite carriers. The simulation results show that the proposed algorithm can achieve effective spectrum resource management and improve data rate while reducing energy consumption and latency. By introducing deep learning into smart agriculture, it can be well used for identifying crop growth processes. However, using deep learning to detect crop growth on mobile devices requires high energy consumption, thereby reducing device runtime. At the same time, implementing deep learning algorithms on remote servers may lead to higher latency and lower quality of service. Therefore, the authors in [\[395\]](#page-61-10) propose a low complexity and low computational delay wheat growth period detection model, and then a task migration algorithm based on DQN algorithm is proposed to minimize the weighted sum of energy consumption and delay in task migration. The experimental results show that the proposed algorithm can reduce energy consumption and improve efficiency. The author in [\[396\]](#page-61-11) proposes a smart agricultural IoT system based on DRL to increase food production and reduce the consumption of freshwater resources. Firstly, the system consists of data acquisition layer, edge computing layer, data transmission layer and cloud computing layer. Secondly, by integrating DRL algorithms into the cloud computing layer, real-time decision-making is provided for the production of smart agriculture, such as determining the required irrigation water amount for crop growth environment. Finally, the author also discusses the potential challenges and development directions of DRL in smart agriculture.

## <span id="page-46-3"></span><span id="page-46-0"></span>*E. Lesson Learned and Future Directions*

This section reviews related applications of cloud-edge intelligent collaboration, such as intelligent transportation systems (ITS), Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT, smart health and digital agriculture. The CETCN can provide flexible and dynamic resource configurations for these emerging applications. Not only that, the introduction of DRL makes the CETCN more intelligent. Specifically, delay-sensitive services need to provide extremely low latency for users, while some traffic-intensive applications require extremely high bandwidth. Although DRL-assisted CETCN in related applications is better than other traditional methods, many problems still need to be solved. Next, we will list several possible research directions for the CETCN in future applications.

- *More emerging applications:* For example, in bearing fault detection, such computationally intensive tasks may require low response latency, low energy consumption, and high processing precision. In addition to traditional performance indicators such as delay and energy consumption, various emerging applications in the future may derive various performance indicators according to different tasks. Flexible indicator configuration and higher-dimensional performance optimization problems will be a potential research direction.
- *Security problem:* Due to the increasing number of various terminal devices, more and more data is generated, collected, and processed at the terminal. Therefore, data security is becoming one of the critical obstacles which affects the further development of the CETCN. As a distributed and secure ledger, blockchain technology has been used by more and more scholars to overcome existing security problems in recent years. With the

pseudonym mechanism of the blockchain, edge nodes can protect privacy well when transmitting data to other nodes other than local nodes. At the same time, since edge computing and blockchain are both distributed solutions, they can be well integrated without causing additional scalability problems.

## <span id="page-46-2"></span>IX. SECURITY ISSUES FOR AI-ENABLED CLOUD-EDGE-TERMINAL COLLABORATIVE NETWORK

<span id="page-46-4"></span>With the continuous improvement of model accuracy, the leakage of personal privacy has become increasingly serious. How to ensure that the training process of artificial intelligence models can comply with data protection regulations and protect personal privacy has become an urgent problem that needs to be solved. Differential privacy is a technique used to protect the privacy of individuals' sensitive information while allowing aggregated statistical analysis on the data. It ensures that individual data points cannot be re-identified, even when combined with other external datasets [\[397\]](#page-61-12). The application of differential privacy to end-edge-cloud collaboration network can play a crucial role in safeguarding users' sensitive information while enabling efficient and secure data processing across the entire network. Therefore, in Section [IX-A,](#page-46-1) we give a simple example of differential privacy. Then, we make a overview of applying differential privacy to end-edge-cloud collaboration to protect users sensitive information in [IX-B.](#page-47-0) Finally, we give the lesson learned and future directions in Section [IX-C.](#page-48-0)

## <span id="page-46-1"></span>*A. A Toy Example*

In the context of end-edge-cloud collaboration network, the collaboration involves multiple entities, such as end-user devices, edge servers, and cloud data centers. These entities exchange data and perform computations collaboratively to provide various services and applications. Differential privacy can be applied at different stages of data processing within this collaboration to protect users' privacy:

- *Data Collection at the End-User Devices:* When sensitive data is collected from end-user devices, differential privacy techniques can be used to add noise to the data before it is transmitted to the edge or cloud. By adding controlled noise, individual data points are obfuscated, ensuring that the original data cannot be inferred from the collected data.
- *Aggregation at the Edge Servers:* In an end-edge-cloud collaboration, edge servers often aggregate data from multiple end-user devices to perform local computations. Differential privacy can be applied to the aggregation process, ensuring that no individual user's data can be distinguished in the aggregated results.
- *Secure Computation in the Cloud:* Differential privacy can also be applied to computations performed in the cloud data centers. When performing joint computations involving data from multiple sources, differential privacy guarantees that the sensitive information of individual users remains protected.

## <span id="page-47-0"></span>*B. Literature Review*

In the following, we will do a brief review on differential privacy in intelligent CETCN.

*1) Differential Privacy in RL:* Local differential privacy (LDP), as a powerful privacy protection tool, breaks the constraints of trusted third parties and allows users to encrypt data locally, providing more powerful privacy protection [\[398\]](#page-61-13). Reinforcement learning agents collect data and learn the strategies through interaction with the environment. However, the data provided by the environment may involve personal information, such as the private layout of the interior. For example, in [\[399\]](#page-61-14), the author proposes environmental dynamic search based on genetic algorithm and candidate inference based on shadow strategy to study whether trained DRL strategies will leak privacy information. They have confirmed that the proposed algorithm can accurately infer floor plans.

<span id="page-47-4"></span><span id="page-47-3"></span><span id="page-47-2"></span>The author in [\[400\]](#page-61-15) proposes a LDP algorithm for reinforcement learning, which achieves good robustness in distributed private environments. Specifically, in the proposed A3C algorithm, randomness is introduced through the Laplace method and random projection method. At the same time, randomness is also introduced in the distribution gradient that satisfies LDP to prevent information leakage. The author in [\[401\]](#page-61-16) studies the problem of random multi-armed bandit (MAB) problem, with the aim of observing the maximum sum of rewards after random destruction. At the same time, they provide an upper bound for the expected regret of any bandit algorithm that incorporates random noise, and provide a lower bound for regret through frequency algorithm and Bayesian algorithm. The experimental results show the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm in recommendation systems that protect user privacy using random perturbations. The author introduces a unified privacy definition for multi-arm bandit algorithms in [\[402\]](#page-61-17) and derives the lower bound of errors for algorithms that meet these definitions. The proof results indicate that the regret of the intelligent agent increases by a multiplication factor that depends on the level of privacy. The author in [\[403\]](#page-61-18) proposes an online DRL algorithm based on differential privacy to address the security issues that may arise during DRL training. Specifically, firstly, Gaussian noise is added to the neural network based on privacy budget, and secondly, an automatic encoder is trained to protect the data in the original environment. At the same time, they also demonstrate the budget formula for online deep learning based on differential privacy. Finally, the experiment shows that the proposed algorithm has good decision-making ability while protecting data privacy.

<span id="page-47-12"></span><span id="page-47-11"></span><span id="page-47-7"></span><span id="page-47-6"></span><span id="page-47-5"></span>*2) Differential Privacy in CETCN Task Offloading and Resource Allocation:* Due to the limited computing power of various sensors in the IIoT scenario, it is necessary to offload tasks on the production line to the edge server. However, uploading data to the edge server poses a privacy breach issue. Therefore, the author [\[404\]](#page-61-19) proposes a DRL algorithm based on LDP to provide personalized privacy protection. Due to the ability of LDP to generate different levels of noise, it can meet various privacy requirements for task offloading. At the same time, the author adds priority experience replay into the algorithm to reduce the impact of noise on the QoS <span id="page-47-10"></span><span id="page-47-9"></span><span id="page-47-8"></span><span id="page-47-1"></span>of offloading tasks. Finally, the simulation results show that the algorithm can achieve good privacy protection ability and cost efficiency. The authors in [\[405\]](#page-61-20) propose a novel online privacy protection offloading mechanism to address the privacy leakage issue in honest but curious MEC server computing offloading, and thus generating real-time and efficient offloading strategies for users. Firstly, they design a DRL based offloading model that allows users to determine satisfactory disturbance offloading ratios based on time-varying channel states, in order to balance user privacy and computational costs. Specifically, in order to protect the real data being offloaded and prevent untrusted MEC servers from leaking user privacy, a range constrained Laplace transform is proposed to blur the original offloading ratio of each user. Finally, a large number of experiments have shown the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm in protecting user privacy. In [\[406\]](#page-61-21), the author not only considers latency and energy consumption, but also considers a privacy protection offloading scheme that minimizes packet loss rate. Specifically, the author describes the dynamic computing offloading problem as an MDP, and then develops an offloading algorithm based on differential privacy DQN to solve the MDP problem. By injecting noise into the generated offloading decisions, the pattern privacy problem is solved, which prevents attackers from inferring queue information and user usage patterns of edge servers through eavesdropping on offloading decisions. In addition, the generation of noise is achieved by modifying the DQN algorithm through a Gaussian process. Finally, the simulation results show that the algorithm outperforms the greedy algorithm and DQN algorithm in terms of performance. Due to the fact that attackers with user history data in ITS can easily infer the real-time location of users, [\[407\]](#page-61-22) proposes a privacy oriented task offloading scheme in ITS to resist attackers' attacks. Firstly, the author defines local computing models, communication models, and privacy loss models, where the privacy loss model is defined as the probability of successful attack by an attacker. Then, the optimal offloading problem is formulated as an MDP and a PPO based DRL algorithm is proposed to obtain the optimal solution. Finally, the simulation results indicate that this method can handle large-scale task offloading problems and achieve good offloading strategies while reducing privacy loss, energy consumption, and latency. The authors in [\[408\]](#page-61-23) propose an offloading scheme for privacy protection in satellite ground collaborative computing. Specifically, this article uses different offloading targets, such as completion time, energy consumption, and reliability, to balance mode privacy and location privacy. Then, the authors introduce a privacy protection algorithm based on DRL to solve the optimal offloading strategy. The experimental results show that the proposed algorithm outperforms the baseline scheme in terms of completion time, energy consumption, and privacy protection level. In order to reduce offloading preference inference attacks in DRL, the authors in [\[409\]](#page-61-24) propose a privacy aware joint task offloading and power optimization scheme to minimize system costs. By incorporating the designed differential privacy mechanism into the process of action selection and policy update in DRL, privacy protection is achieved. Theoretical analysis shows that this method can enable each vehicle to make offloading decisions without compromising privacy and achieve Nash equilibrium. Finally, the effectiveness of this method in protecting privacy and improving cost-effectiveness is verified through a large number of experiments.

<span id="page-48-2"></span><span id="page-48-1"></span>*3) Differential Privacy in CETCN Caching:* The authors in [\[410\]](#page-61-25) propose a distributed caching and routing strategy with privacy protection mechanism. Firstly, they propose a distributed Gaussian Seidel iteration method to achieve global optimization of caching and routing problem. Secondly, they design a differential privacy mechanism to protect routing information in the base station and analyze the performance of the proposed algorithm while ensuring performance. Finally, simulation of real-world data demonstrates that the proposed algorithm can significantly reduce costs. In order to cache content without compromising user privacy. The authors in [\[411\]](#page-61-26) propose a content caching scheme based on LDP. Firstly, content providers collect LDP content preferences of specific users and provide economic incentives to access points that provide content to maximize the benefits of content providers. Secondly, by using an optimized local hash method to add noise to the preferred content, and using the reference distribution constructed from noisy content preference data to predict the popularity of the content. Finally, they provide a data-driven cache benefit scheme and verify its effectiveness through simulation. Due to insufficient state collection between edge servers and mobile users, as well as the presence of malicious attacks, existing edge caching schemes not only struggle to improve video experience quality, but also easily leak personal privacy. To address this issue, the author in [\[412\]](#page-61-27) proposes a video caching framework based on privacy preserving Q-learning. Firstly, the author uses LDP schemes to protect users' location and preferences. Then, a data aggregation model is proposed to balance aggregation accuracy and privacy. At the same time, the popularity of videos in a certain region is obtained by calculating the service model, and then edge caching with low latency and high hit rate is achieved by setting a caching objective function. Finally, a large number of simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme can protect user privacy and improve the quality of user video experience.

<span id="page-48-3"></span>*4) Differential Privacy in CETCN Mobility Management:* Vehicle trajectory data is crucial for location-based services, however, containing private information about families and individuals can raise concerns about data security. By utilizing differential privacy, we can solve this problem by generating synthetic trajectories from raw sensitive data while protecting personal privacy. The existing synthetic trajectories often contain aggregation level information, which loses personal level movement patterns. Therefore, in [\[413\]](#page-61-28), a new private trajectory solution is proposed, which integrates the public geographic structure of the target area into the private movement trajectory to capture more accurate movement patterns while preserving aggregation level movement behavior. A large number of simulation results indicate that the proposed scheme can improve data utility by 37%. Users' mobile data may contain trade secrets of mobile providers (MP), and mobile data can also provide insights for municipal authorities <span id="page-48-6"></span><span id="page-48-5"></span>(MA) on how to operate, regulate, and improve transportation networks. Due to data security considerations, MP may not be willing to provide datasets to MA. In [\[414\]](#page-61-29), in order to provide MA with insights on mobile data, a differential privacy based interaction protocol between MP and MA is proposed, and MP does not need to disclose trade secrets. Specifically, the first step is to generate encryption commitments through Merkel commitments and aggregated traffic metrics, while the second step checks the correctness of the received data through the use of commitments and zero knowledge proofs. The author also provides a private version of the protocol suitable for large query mechanisms, which can be easily extended to scenarios with multiple MPs through secure multi-party computation. In order to optimize task allocation in mobile crowdsourcing without exposing user location information, the authors in [\[415\]](#page-61-30) propose a geographically obfuscated location privacy protection task allocation framework. Specifically, they enable participants to obfuscate their reported locations without the need for any actual third-party trusted entities, while ensuring differential and distorted privacy. In addition, under the constraints of differential privacy and distorted privacy, a MINLP problem is constructed to minimize the expected travel distance of the operator. Finally, a large number of simulation experiments have demonstrated that the proposed scheme can shorten the average transmission distance under the same level of privacy protection. With the development of smart cities, better location-based services are provided by analyzing people's trajectory data. However, the release of raw data poses a serious security threat to individuals. Privacy protection frameworks based on differential privacy often assume that individuals have the same preferences for privacy. The authors in [\[416\]](#page-61-31) propose a personalized differential privacy publishing mechanism for trajectory data based on individual privacy requirements. It utilizes Hilbert curves to extract the distribution characteristics of each trajectory data, and proposes personalized privacy protect algorithms for trajectories with different privacy preferences. Finally, experiments on realworld trajectory datasets confirm that the proposed mechanism can better balance data privacy and data utility.

## <span id="page-48-7"></span><span id="page-48-0"></span>*C. Lesson Learned and Future Directions*

Differential privacy has emerged as a promising technique for privacy protection in various data analysis and sharing scenarios. As the field of data privacy and security continues to evolve, several future directions and advancements are expected in the area of differential privacy:

- <span id="page-48-4"></span>• *Improved Privacy Guarantees:* Future research will focus on refining and strengthening the privacy guarantees provided by differential privacy. Efforts will be made to reduce the amount of noise introduced in the data while still maintaining a high level of privacy protection.
- *Dynamic Privacy Budget Management:* Managing the privacy budget is critical in differential privacy. Future developments may explore dynamic mechanisms that allocate privacy budget based on the specific data and analysis requirements to achieve better utility while preserving privacy.

- *Privacy for Complex Data Types:* Current differential privacy techniques are well-suited for numerical data, but extending these methods to handle complex data types like text, images, and graphs remains a challenge. Future research will focus on developing privacy-preserving mechanisms for these data types.
- *Privacy in Machine Learning:* Integrating differential privacy with machine learning algorithms is an active area of research. Future directions will explore ways to protect individual data points during the training process while still achieving accurate and useful models.
- *Privacy in Real-Time and Streaming Data:* As data processing becomes more real-time and streaming-oriented, future research will focus on developing differential privacy techniques for streaming data that can handle continuous data updating and adapt to changing data distributions.
- *Federated Differential Privacy:* Federated learning and privacy are closely related concepts. Future developments may explore combining federated learning with differential privacy to achieve privacy-preserving collaborative machine learning across multiple entities.

## X. STANDARDIZATION EFFORTS OF EDGE INTELLIGENCE

Standardization refers to the formulation, publication, and implementation of standards for repetitive things and concepts in social practices such as economy, technology, science, and management, in order to achieve the best order and social benefits. The important significance of standardization work lies in improving the practicality of products, processes, and services, preventing trade barriers, and promoting technological cooperation. Moving from 5G to 6G networks, data-driven machine learning and artificial intelligence have become increasingly important for real-time applications, including but not limited to personal intelligence devices and smart factories. At the same time, how to effectively run AI applications at the edge of the network has become a research hotspot. There is currently no unified definition of edge intelligence in the industrial and academic communities. In 2019, the International Electrotechnical Commission defined edge intelligence as the ability to use machine learning algorithms for data collection, storage, analysis, and aggregation at the edges [\[417\]](#page-61-32). Therefore, in this subsection, we will review relevant standardization works and technical challenges of the edge intelligence.

<span id="page-49-0"></span>The standardization process of edge intelligence is being promoted by ITU-T. ITU-T is responsible for studying technical, operating and tariff questions and issuing recommendations on them with a view to standardizing telecommunications on a worldwide basis [\[418\]](#page-61-33). Due to the data loss and network delay caused by the network bottleneck between the terminal and the cloud device, ITU-T suggests to provide enhanced networking capabilities for emerging services and applications through artificial intelligence technology and edge computing technology [\[419\]](#page-61-34), [\[420\]](#page-61-35). For the architecture development of intelligent edge computing, the recommendation [\[421\]](#page-61-36) specifies the signaling <span id="page-49-4"></span>architecture, protocol interface, protocol process and message format of intelligent edge computing based on microservices. Furthermore, edge cloud platform services can assist in smart agriculture and achieve edge cloud assisted smart agriculture systems. Reference [\[422\]](#page-61-37) defines the data management interface of intelligent agricultural services based on intelligent edge computing. Recently, the *Third Generation Partner Program* (3GPP) has shown increasing interest in incorporating artificial intelligence into its 5G system, and has identified and reported functional support for the 5G system in recent technical specification documents [\[423\]](#page-61-38).

## <span id="page-49-5"></span>*A. Standardization Efforts of IEEE*

<span id="page-49-7"></span><span id="page-49-6"></span>The IEEE has been actively involved in the development of standards related to edge intelligence and edge computing. Standard [\[424\]](#page-61-39) defines the OpenFog reference architecture, which is a framework aimed at supporting fog computing, including aspects related to edge intelligence. Specifically, in this standard, four aspects related to edge intelligence are defined. Firstly, it defines the ability to perform data analysis and apply machine learning algorithms at the edge of the network. Secondly, the machine learning model can be deployed on edge devices. The third is the ability to perform real-time data processing. Finally, there can be inheritance between edge intelligence and cloud resources to meet complex analysis and data aggregation. Apart from realtime analysis, [\[425\]](#page-61-40) highlights the possibility of executing complex computational tasks on mobile devices, rather than relying solely on remote cloud servers or data centers. While this standard does not directly address edge intelligence, the concepts related to task offloading are consistent with edge computing. To be specific, edge intelligence may involve dynamically decide whether to execute a task locally on the mobile device or offload it to a nearby edge server or cloud resource based on factors like device resources, network conditions, and user preferences. In [\[426\]](#page-61-41), IEEE defines the essential concepts and terminology for the IoT. Within the context of IoT, edge intelligence is an integral aspect where data is processed and analyzed at the edge of the network, which is closer to the data source. The standard may acknowledge the importance of edge intelligence in IoT scenarios, where devices and sensors generate vast amounts of data that need to be analyzed, interpreted, and acted upon in real-time at the network edge. Although standard [\[427\]](#page-61-42) does not focus on edge intelligence directly, time-sensitive network aims to provide deterministic and low-latency communication in Ethernet-based networks, which is critical for supporting time-critical applications such as those used in edge computing and edge intelligence scenarios.

## <span id="page-49-9"></span><span id="page-49-8"></span><span id="page-49-1"></span>*B. Standardization Efforts of ITU*

<span id="page-49-3"></span><span id="page-49-2"></span>The International Telecommunication Union (ITU) has been actively involved in various standardization efforts related to edge computing and edge intelligence. Although the ITU does not have specific standards solely dedicated to edge intelligence, we summarize the standards of edge computing which involve edge intelligence.

<span id="page-50-2"></span><span id="page-50-1"></span>In [\[428\]](#page-61-43), it points out the requirements for the deployment of edge computing which including the capability of edge intelligence. This standard highlights the importance of edge intelligence in supporting real-time data processing and decision-making at the network edge, enabling timely responses to time-critical applications. In addition, this standard also addresses the need for inter-operability between different edge computing components, including edge intelligence modules, to facilitate seamless integration and data exchange. Standard [\[429\]](#page-61-44) addresses the essential architectural aspects of edge computing, including highlighting the distribution of computation, storage, and data processing capabilities should be closer to the edge of the network. This standard discusses the orchestration of services and applications within the edge computing architecture, where edge intelligence can dynamically allocate resources and manage services based on changing conditions.

## *C. Technical Challenges and Requirements*

In this subsection, we will discuss several technical challenges and requirements of edge intelligence.

- *Limited Computing Resources*: Edge devices often have limited processing power, memory, and energy resources. Executing sophisticated machine learning algorithms and artificial intelligence tasks on resource-constrained edge devices can be challenging.
- *Data Privacy and Security*: Edge devices generate and process sensitive data, raising concerns about data privacy and security. Safeguarding data at the edge and ensuring secure communication between edge devices and cloud resources are essential challenges.
- *Latency and Real-time Processing*: Edge intelligence applications often require real-time processing and quick decision-making. Ensuring low latency and timely response is a significant challenge.
- *Scalability and Management*: Deploying and managing a large number of edge devices in diverse environments pose challenges related to scalability, orchestration, and configuration management.
- *Cost and Energy Efficiency*: Edge intelligence solutions must consider the cost and energy implications of performing computation and analytics at the edge versus offloading tasks to the cloud.

## *D. Edge Intelligence for 6G*

It is expected that edge intelligence will play a significant role in the development of 6G networks. As with previous generations of wireless communication technologies, 6G is expected to support an even higher level of data throughput, lower latency, and increased connectivity. To achieve these objectives, edge computing and intelligence are likely to be essential components of the 6G ecosystem. Some potential aspects of edge intelligence could relevant to 6G.

• *Ultra-Low Latency*: Edge intelligence can enable realtime data processing and decision-making at the network edge, contributing to the achievement of ultra-low latency requirements in 6G networks.

- *Enhanced User Experience*: Edge intelligence can personalize services and applications for individual users based on real-time data analysis, leading to enhanced user experiences in 6G networks.
- *Distributed Machine Learning*: Edge intelligence can enable collaborative machine learning and distributed training at the edge, improving the efficiency and scalability of machine learning models in 6G environments.

## XI. CHALLENGES AND FUTURE DIRECTIONS

<span id="page-50-0"></span>In this section, we discuss challenges and future directions of applying AI to the CETCN.

## *A. DRL Training Problem*

Most research assumes DRL agents are trained in a centralized manner. However, in the CETCN, the DRL agent can be implemented in a cloud server and this centralized training in a cloud server may lead to the following problems: 1) The training data needs to be transmitted to a central server, which makes users more concerned about the leakage of private data. At the same time, transmitting massive data also adds burdens to the communication network and consumes huge amount of energy in general. 2) The limited computing power of the terminals leads to the failure of the training process. Therefore, how to appropriately train the DRL agents need further research.

<span id="page-50-3"></span>Federated learning is a promising method to overcome the resource limitation of terminals and release the privacy concerns. First, a well-designed computational offloading framework can enable the training process to be performed in resource-constrained terminal devices [\[430\]](#page-61-45). Secondly, federated learning can also be integrated into the distributed training process, which can obtain knowledge from a large amount of data and ensure user privacy at the same time [\[431\]](#page-61-46).

## <span id="page-50-4"></span>*B. The Integration of Emerging Communication Technologies and DRL*

<span id="page-50-6"></span><span id="page-50-5"></span>The rapid growth of a variety of emerging applications is driving the development of next-generation wireless technologies, including massive multiple-input multiple-output (Massive MIMO), millimeter-wave communications and reflected intelligent surface (RIS) [\[432\]](#page-62-0). However, achieving resource optimization in such a time-varying and unpredictable environment is a very challenging task [\[433\]](#page-62-1). DRL is seen as a viable tool to solve dynamic resource optimization. However, with the deployment of ultra-dense networks, the action space will become larger and larger, resulting in the curse of dimensionality. Therefore, it is necessary to find new ways to solve the dimensionality explosion problems, such as multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) and game theory [\[434\]](#page-62-2).

## <span id="page-50-7"></span>*C. The Synchronization Issue of Multi-Agent*

<span id="page-50-8"></span>The centralized training of MARL puts forward higher requirements for the synchronization of states between agents. In [\[435\]](#page-62-3), an ideal collection framework has been proposed to synchronize the state information of all the agents. However, the CETCN is distributed and decentralized in a wide range, and it is difficult for the network vendors to obtain such the collection the global information simultaneously to learn the policy. Furthermore, the unreliability of wireless communication links, such as V2V links and IIoT links, is also an main obstacle to realize the synchronization among agents. Because of these factors, the agents have to learn from past state information and thus inevitably lead to a non-optimal policy.

## *D. Mixed Reward Design and Multi-Objective Optimization*

The design of the return function is crucial to the learning process of the agent. A well-designed return function can greatly increase the efficiency of agent learning. However, the CETCN involves multiple network entities with inconsistent objectives. For example, users want to get the ultimate service experience, while content providers want to get the highest revenue from users. Designing effective reward functions for different entities in the network is still an open issue. The reviewed literature typically define a global reward for all the network vendors or a local reward for an individual agent. Both schemes have their own advantages and disadvantages. The global return can encourage the cooperation between network entities, but it will also generate lazy network entities. At the same time, designing the local return function will make each agent in the network pay all their efforts to obtain the maximum benefits for themselves, but it will also cause the competition of agents for the limited resources in the network. Due to these reasons, the mixed reward has been proposed to improve the learning performance of agents [\[436\]](#page-62-4). Furthermore, it is common to design a conflict reward for the CETCN, for example, maximizing the revenue for the content providers while minimizing the service delay of the users.

## *E. Multi-Agent State Information Extraction*

In the MARL framework, each agent makes decisions by using the past state information. However, due to the limited processing capacity of various edge devices, the state information obtained by the agent is often not preprocessed, and resulting in the low learning efficiency of multi-agent reinforcement learning. There exist several methods to extract the state information, such as GNN [\[437\]](#page-62-5) and attention mechanism [\[438\]](#page-62-6). However, these state information extraction technologies usually need to collect information from other agents frequently, thus increasing the burden of communication links. Therefore, it is still an urgent problem to design effective state information extraction technology for the CETCN.

## <span id="page-51-2"></span>*F. Large Scale Heterogeneous Agents Cooperation*

The definition of mean-field Nash equilibrium discussed above corresponds to non-cooperative game, in which each agent aims to maximize its own utility which trying to join in the behavior of other selfish agents. However, in the CETCN, edge nodes sometimes tries to cooperative to minimize the total transmission delay of all end users which in line with cooperative game. At the same time, because the mean field control assumes that players are isomorphic, that is, only by studying a representative player can learn the optimal strategy. However, this assumption is impractical in the cloud edge collaboration system. For example, there are multiple edge computing devices in the edge system, the computing capacity and cache capacity of these edge computing devices are often very different. If the calculation delay per unit time is taken as the return function, the return function of each edge device will be different, and the whole system will no longer be homogeneous. Therefore, reinforcement learning methods in large-scale heterogeneous multi-agent environment need to be further studied.

## *G. Quantum Computing-Assisted CETCN (QCCETCN)*

<span id="page-51-3"></span>Quantum computing is a revolutionary technology. It uses quantum mechanism to solve the problems that traditional computers cannot solve, such as traditional encryption and decryption problems. It has greatly influence on existing technology fields, such as artificial intelligence (AI) [\[439\]](#page-62-7), security [\[440\]](#page-62-8), and finance [\[441\]](#page-62-9). By deploying quantum computers at the edge of the network, users' tasks will be processed faster, so that users can obtain the ultimate service experience from quantum computer providers.

<span id="page-51-7"></span><span id="page-51-6"></span><span id="page-51-5"></span><span id="page-51-4"></span><span id="page-51-0"></span>Although quantum computing is in its infancy, the QCCETCN still has the following advantages compared with traditional CETCN. On the one hand, quantum computer can perform complex calculations while transmitting and processing data. For example, the Google Sycamore nuclear machine can complete a task within 200 seconds, while a traditional computer takes 10000 years to complete the task [\[442\]](#page-62-10). On the other hand, DRL algorithms need a heavy learning phase, which require huge computing capacities [\[443\]](#page-62-11), [\[444\]](#page-62-12). Therefore, the computing power for processing massive amount of data is hard to reach for traditional CETCN. But fortunately, quantum machine learning (QML) utilizes the principles of quantum mechanics to develop new algorithms that can solve complex problems in a very fast manner [\[445\]](#page-62-13). In this sense, quantum computing is suitable for the CETCN to accomplish computationally heavy and real-time applications.

## <span id="page-51-8"></span><span id="page-51-1"></span>*H. The Convergence Proof of Applying DRL in CETCN*

<span id="page-51-9"></span>Deep reinforcement learning obtains the optimal policy through iterative interaction with the complex and dynamic environment, but this optimization algorithm that obtains the optimal solution through interactive learning leads to difficulties in proving the convergence of the algorithm. In the existing literature that use DRL to solve the optimization problem in CETCN, few of them mention the proof of convergence [\[446\]](#page-62-14), and most of them illustrate the convergence of the algorithm through simulation. It is generally not convincing to illustrate the convergence of the algorithm through simulation. Therefore, when using the DRL algorithm to solve the CETCN problem, a more rigorous mathematical proof of convergence needs to be further investigated.

## *I. Lightweight Privacy Protection Schemes*

Massive emerging applications in CETCN that need to exchange sensitive data. For example, household electricity consumption reports contain massive private information. This privacy information needs to be accessed by different professions and organizations, including electrical distribution personnel, and employees of the national grid. In this case, easy access to data is required in order to make real-time decisions. At the same time, data access should be protected to prevent unauthorized modification. However, existing methods to protect data privacy, such as blockchain and asymmetric key encryption, are computationally intensive and greatly reduce the ability to interact in real-time when identifying access requests and modifications to authorized data [\[447\]](#page-62-15), [\[448\]](#page-62-16), [\[449\]](#page-62-17). Therefore, it is necessary to develop lightweight data protection methods that can support real-time interaction while ensuring secure data management.

## XII. CONCLUSION

<span id="page-52-28"></span><span id="page-52-27"></span>As an emerging network paradigm, the CETCN exploits advanced communication technology to enable the system to collect, exchange, analyze and process data at the terminal layer, edge layer and cloud layer, thus solving the problem of the high latency and high energy consumption in the traditional networks. At the same time, AI also provides the CETCN with high-value and real-time decision-making ability in an unprecedented way. In this paper, we provide a review of the literature on AI-enabled CETCN, especially in collaborative task offloading, resource allocation, caching, mobility management, and their applications in emerging scenarios. Firstly, we review the related concepts in the AI-enabled CETCN and provide a comprehensive tutorial for task offloading, resource allocation, collaborative caching and mobility management and give two use cases in AI-enabled collaborative cloud-edgeterminal collaborative network. Secondly, we point out a few research challenges and future directions in each part of this tutorial. Finally, we highlight the research challenges of reinforcement learning and point out several possible development directions in the convergence of emerging technologies and the CETCN.
